About:

### PULSE — Predict Risk Intelligence Terminal

**Track: DeepBook | Network: Sui Testnet

**

### Overview

PULSE is a risk-aware trading terminal for DeepBook Predict that transforms raw protocol data into actionable risk intelligence. It visualizes volatility surfaces, scores protocol safety, and prevents unsafe trades before execution.

Key Features

• Live SVI Surface Visualization
• Deterministic Risk Guardian
• Mint Protection Layer
• PLP Vault Monitoring
• Settled-Redeem Keeper Feed

Risk Guardian Factors

Oracle Freshness (25%), Vault Utilization (20%), Liquidity Depth (20%), Surface Integrity (20%), Oracle Lifecycle (15%)

Hard Blocking Conditions

Stale Oracle, Settled Oracle, Inactive Oracle, Vault Utilization ≥ 78%

Innovation

A reusable Risk Guardian framework providing quantitative risk scoring, explainable decisions, real-time monitoring, and deterministic execution safeguards.

Submission Highlights

✓ Live SVI volatility analytics
✓ Arbitrage detection engine
✓ Deterministic risk scoring
✓ DeepBook Predict integration
✓ Risk-gated trading workflow
✓ Real-time operational visibility

Tagline

See the Surface. Trust the Trade.

About:

Provable yield on Sui. Every number a vault tells you, you can verify on-chain.

Every DeFi vault asks you to trust a number — a NAV, an APY, a collateral value computed by some off-chain bot you can't audit. Floe makes the number prove itself.

We run vault valuation inside a Nautilus hardware enclave, which signs each result with a cryptographic attestation of exactly what code ran. A Move contract on Sui then verifies that signature on-chain before any figure can move money. Reported NAV and proven floor, side by side — and the proven floor is the one the contract enforces. Click any number, land on its proof.

Built natively on DeepBook:

- Earn — curated vaults deploy into DeepBook Predict, lending, and Cetus, each with a hardware-proven floor, not a marketing APY.

- Borrow — lock vault shares and borrow against collateral whose value the contract verifies against an enclave signature. Trustless leverage on a trustless mark.

- Surface Studio — a live 3D implied-vol surface reconstructed from DeepBook Predict's on-chain SVI oracles. Real skew, real term structure, zero fabrication.

- Deploy — ship your own vault with the same SDK we built on.

The moat is three Sui primitives doing real work: Nautilus (attested compute), Walrus (verifiable storage), Seal (gated data) — wrapped in a non-custodial, capability-gated design with an on-chain circuit breaker.

About:

Wick Markets is a suite of fast, provably-fair on-chain trading games on Sui. Pick a mode and you're trading in seconds — no wallet and no popups, since a one-tap faucet drips testnet funds for you. Wick Pro is a one-tap options game: tap UP or DOWN on SUI, BTC, or DEEP, watch a single big P&L tick live off a real DeepBook order-book price, and close early or auto-settle in 60 seconds. Ride is a press-and-hold "touch" game: hold the chart and ride the candle in real time — touch the barrier and win, release early for a cashout, get no touch and the vault keeps your stake.

What makes Wick different is that the chart itself comes from Sui's on-chain randomness. Every candle is drawn from a sui::random draw committed on-chain, so the price you trade against is generated by the chain, not a server. Because of that, every round is replayable bit-for-bit: our Move path generator has an exact TypeScript twin (verified against the chain over 10,000 vectors in CI), so anyone can replay a finished round from its on-chain keys — in one command or in-browser at /verify — and prove the house never cheated. The house edge is a single published, deterministic rule (+3.93%), not hidden math.

Under the hood, Wick uses the full Sui stack. Wick Pro reads a live DeepBook v3 order-book mid and derives volatility from the live trade tape, then prices real Black-Scholes options through its own engine — a real venue, not a mocked feed. The on-chain side is 26 Move modules (segment market, a loss-recycling LP vault, a fee router, and probability-weighted exposure caps) backed by 592 passing Move tests, plus a typed SDK, a permissionless keeper, trader bots, and a no-wallet faucet.

Wick wins because it is provably fair, financially honest, and a genuinely new kind of game — a position that exists only while you hold the screen, painted tick-by-tick from on-chain randomness — shipped as a polished, 60fps, mobile-first product anyone can pick up and play.

Deployment — Sui testnet. Package v4.26: 0x1fdf784743d82c000e84154506e21daedc45bf241818fef6b28635e99e815924. Live: [wick-markets.vercel.app](http://wick-markets.vercel.app). Repo: github.com/SeamMoney/sui-options.

About:

[Video in README.md]

- **Business Pain Points**: Prediction markets have seen explosive growth but remain structurally inefficient. Standard platforms quote simple binary probabilities, failing to price volatility smiles or term structures. This creates significant implied volatility discrepancies (often 20–40 vol points) across fragmented venues.
.

- **Solution**: SviMarlin is a high-performance quant trading engine. It stream-solves the on-chain SVI (Stochastic Volatility Inspired) surface from DeepBook Predict, aggregates prices from external venues (Hyperliquid, Limitless), and executes atomic spread trades on Sui while delta-hedging residual exposure.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Implied Vol Solver**: Back-solves SVI parameters from DeepBook Predict's oracle updates.

- **Multi-Venue Pricing Engine**: Translates binary options from multiple venues into implied volatilities.

- **Atomic Spread execution**: Mints underpriced binary options on Sui and sells overpriced legs on external venues.

- **Risk Mitigator**: Incorporates Kelly sizing, Pyth price verifications, and delta-hedging perp strategies.

About:

Sentinel is a niche prediction market on Sui that doubles as parametric flight-delay insurance. Travelers buy a fixed USDC payout for a specific flight; if it lands more than 45 minutes late or is cancelled, they are paid automatically — minutes after the gate, no

 claim form, no human adjuster.

On the other side of the same market, underwriters supply USDC to a shared vault and earn the premium spread when flights land on time. Settlement is parametric: an AeroAPI feed turns each flight outcome into a single on-chain scalar — delay in minutes, with a sentinel value above any plausible delay for cancellation — and a single (strike = 45, is_up = true) binary captures both the delayed and cancelled cases.

Two structural problems collapse into one mechanism:

- Travel insurance stops being adversarial,

- DeFi yield gets an off-chain anchor.

We chose Sui because of DeepBook Predict (DBP), Mysten's Move-native prediction-market framework. Sentinel was prototyped on Soroban and then ported across Solana, Base, and Hedera; every chain forced us to hand-roll the same six primitives, and the homegrown vault was sixty percent of the audit cost.

DBP ships exactly the three primitives a parametric protocol needs -

-  a shared underwriter vault with worst-case solvency,.

-  a per-event oracle,

-  per-user position storage .

We use DBP's architecture and deploy our own instance. our Move.toml pulls DBP's code as a dependency, and bootstrap_dbp.ts creates our Predict<MOCK_DUSDC> vault, our OracleSVICap, and our per-flight oracles, all held by the Sentinel keeper. On top of that we ship ~600 lines of Move (four small modules) and a two-layer AI pricer: a back-looking AI model trained on five years of US Bureau of Transportation on-time data — the base layer of a real, non-emissions underwriter yield — and a forward-looking Grok agent that watches live web signal.

About:

01:53





### Claude responded: Cerida

### Cerida

**A professional-grade, fully customizable prediction market terminal built on Sui.**

Cerida sits on top of DeepBook Predict and wraps it in three distinct trading products binary/range options, leveraged positions ("Turbo Tickets"), and window bets all accessible through a single unified vault. Traders can also combine legs from any product into multi-leg combos, similar to how options desks construct spreads, condors, and diagonal strategies.

The interface is **fully modular**: panels can be added, removed, and rearranged to match your workflow. Pull up the IV surface, GEX profile, live price chart, positions blotter, and trade terminal in whatever layout suits you  or strip it down to just the payoff grid and a single order entry panel. It's a trading environment you configure once and work from, not a fixed dashboard you navigate around.

What makes it interesting is the architecture underneath. Rather than a simple AMM, Cerida uses a **request/execute keeper pattern**: user intent is queued on-chain, a keeper executes it against the live Predict protocol, and all fund flows are governed by on-chain invariants the keeper cannot override. The keeper can route capital but cannot steal it.

The leverage model is particularly elegant **fully-reserved synthetic leverage** where the pool is the counterparty, not a lender. No debt objects. The math bounds the worst-case outcome for both sides at open time, making bad debt structurally impossible.

The analytics run deep: a live IV surface rendered in 3D, a GEX profile panel showing net gamma exposure by strike, a payoff/edge grid for window bets with live multipliers across expiries and bands, and a trade terminal handling binary options, range mints, and leveraged positions in one place.

It reads less like a DeFi app and more like a Bloomberg terminal that happens to settle on-chain and one you can configure exactly the way you want it.

About:

[Video Link in [README.md](http://README.md)]

- **Business Pain Points**: Prediction markets like Polymarket are cluttered with non-crypto topics. Crypto-natives lack a specialised venue focused purely on trading narrative momentum, whilst DeepBook Predict lacks a consumer-facing product to drive mass retail volume.
.

- **Solution**: ShoalPulse fuses real-time narrative sentiment with Sui's volatility surface. It aggregates DeFi TVL, social signal, and market probability into an on-chain `AttentionIndex`, translating narrative momentum into curated prediction suggestions routed to DeepBook Predict.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Narrative Heatmap (**`AttentionIndex`**)**: Dynamic on-chain tracker for crypto-sector momentum.

- **Curation-to-Trade Routing**: Curates binary betting suggestions based on narrative trends and routes trades to DeepBook.

- **Walrus ProfileNFTs**: Gamifies trading with mutable, evolving Seahorse NFTs stored on Walrus.

- **Sub-5-Second Onboarding**: Leverages zkLogin and sponsored gas to remove wallet and funding friction.

About:

Sentinel is short-window parametric crash insurance for Bitcoin, built on DeepBook Predict (Sui). You enter how much BTC you hold, receive a single premium quote priced off the live SVI volatility surface, sign one transaction, and get a receipt. If BTC settles at or below your trigger price (default −2%) before the selected oracle expiry, a keeper pays you out automatically in dUSDC — no claims, no counterparty chase. Under the hood, each policy is a single well-priced mint of a deep out-of-the-money DOWN binary. Payouts are all-or-nothing and the coverage window equals the oracle expiry; it's parametric crash protection, not regulated insurance.

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/5542bf8a-2b51-47d6-b0db-16b5d079db8c/70af045e-888c-487c-bad1-4efcf0a42556.png

About:

**PredictPing** is a consumer-social entry point for DeepBook Predict: a Telegram bot (@predictonsuibot) plus a live web dashboard at `https://predictping-sui.vercel.app`.

Users mint real BTC binary positions with SVI fair-value context, copy-trade leaders, run Stratos-style multi-leg templates, and compete in on-chain tournaments. A 24/7 autonomous Kelly vol-edge agent signs its own `predict::mint` txs under a Move-enforced daily cap, with an optional on-chain BSM delta-hedge path via DeepBook DBTC/DBUSDC.

**Why it is built**

DeepBook Predict exposes sub-hour BTC binaries settled by on-chain SVI oracles. The protocol path requires object IDs, PTB construction, and a PredictManager. That blocks retail and social participation. PredictPing collapses the flow to one Telegram line (`/up 65k 15m 100`) or a three-field web mint, while surfacing SVI edge before every bet. Copy-trade and leaderboard ranking add a social layer missing from terminal-only quant demos in this track.

**Features**

1. Telegram consumer bot (`@predictonsuibot`)
One line replaces terminal PTB wiring: `/up 65k 15m 1` returns an SVI Fair-Value Edge Report, then a real `predict::mint` digest.

2. SVI edge before every bet
SDK fits the on-chain OracleSVI surface and computes quoted price vs fair value with a fixed `normcdf` (unit tested). Bot and web show edge % before the user signs.

"We surface mispricing from the same SVI params Predict settles on, not a black box."

3. Move `agent_budget` daily cap in the same PTB as mint
`agent_budget::spend_under_cap` runs first, then `predict::mint` in one atomic digest (`57sRQv4M…`: SpendRecorded + PositionMinted). Over-cap aborts with `EDailyCapExceeded` code 1 before mint (`EgTkK97s…`). The ceiling is Move-enforced, not SQLite or agent honor system.

4. Copy-trade from on-chain leaderboard
Leaderboard ranks traders by realized PnL from PositionRedeemed/Minted events only. Web Copy reads the leader's latest non-expired position and builds a follower mint at fixed 1 dUSDC stake. Reference pair: leader `713vsQAD…`, follower `AhMnEsAg…`. Honest caveat: relationship is not an on-chain event; two independent mints prove each side.

5. Strategy templates: bull ladder and strangle
Stratos-class multi-leg templates execute as one atomic two-leg PTB (`execute_bull_ladder`, `execute_strangle`). Default 0.5 dUSDC per leg, total stake shown before click. One wallet sign, two PositionMinted events, strikes snapped to oracle ticks around ATM.

6. Autonomous Kelly agent (24/7)
Agent wallet `0x4dbd…2d15` signs its own Kelly-sized mints under the same daily cap. Prod hero card pulses live mint rows; every digest links to Suiscan. Qualification gate closed: real BTC UP and DOWN on Predict testnet, not mock feed data.

7. Delta-hedge PTB (`2Rdcbofp…`)

Submitted PTB combines cap-guarded spend + DeepBook `place_limit_order` for BSM delta hedge. PTB construction and command trace.

Testnet DBTC/DBUSDC pool lacks DEEP-price oracle data (`deep_price::ENoDataPoints`); hedge path is built and on-chain, fully working.

8. On-chain tournament
Tournament module enforces winner is a registered entrant, not the organizer (`DnFGZeZT…`, winner `0x349af70d…`). On-chain guarantees membership + argmax; scores are organizer-attested from public events. Prize coin transfer visible on settle tx.

About:

**LoopVault** is the consumer trading terminal for DeepBook Predict. 40,000+ joined the Predict waitlist with no usable way to trade — and naked one-tap bets are dangerous. LoopVault makes a Predict trade one tap, ~6 seconds, no seed phrase (zkLogin) and gasless (Enoki): a single user-signed PTB atomically opens a Predict volatility/directional position AND delta-hedges it on DeepBook Spot, sealed by a no-abilities SafeMint hot-potato that re-derives the realized cost and oracle freshness ON-CHAIN and rolls the whole trade back unless it lands inside a fresh-oracle window within your max-loss cap. Robinhood/Polymarket-grade UX for the only on-chain vol-surface options primitive on Sui — a real Block Scholes SVI surface, genuine N(d2) digital pricing + forward-delta hedge, a live mark-to-market share card with a copy-trade loop, and a config-only mainnet toggle for Predict mainnet day-one.

About:

DeepBook Predict gives Sui on-chain prediction markets with fixed premium and payout, settled by a price oracle. CallIt is the consumer app on top of it — five surfaces over the same primitive:

- Trade: strikes, ranges, expiries and live quotes in a pro terminal, or Yes/No in Simple mode. Premium and payout are fixed before you confirm.

- Arena: creators bond capital and post a public call. Others Back the same side or Fade the opposite; each opens a native Predict position. Calls carry a track record and a leaderboard.

- Earn: supply DUSDC to the PLP pool and earn fees from the flow. This is the liquidity every call settles against.

- Strategies: deposit into automated vaults (covered PLP, ladders, collars) that trade the book and settle each round on-chain.

- Keeper: a permissionless settler anyone can run; it redeems unclaimed winning positions and earns a reward. No privileged operator.

Every Arena position is a native Predict position and every vault deposit is book liquidity, so CallIt composes DeepBook Predict rather than competing with it — the app sends volume and liquidity back to the protocol.

Live on Sui testnet today: contracts, indexer, identity, and all five surfaces.

What's built

- 8 Move packages published on Sui testnet: Arena, a base vault with a reusable withdrawal queue, 5 strategy vaults, and keeper rewards.

- All five surfaces reading live on-chain data, plus Portfolio.

- Full on-chain Arena flow with DUSDC: launch → back → fade → settle → claim bond / claim payout.

- Backend indexes the Arena over Sui's gRPC checkpoint stream (subscribeCheckpoints + backfill + idle-watchdog, not JSON-RPC, which Sui is deprecating). [Dynamic.xyz](http://Dynamic.xyz) login issues a backend session.

- Operator runs strategy rounds; the keeper redeems on-chain.

- Full stack in Docker Compose, with one deployment manifest driving every service config.

It is not an AI chatbot or a mock dashboard; it is a DeepBook-native terminal for market intelligence, guarded execution, and verifiable proof.

Predict Studio is the structured-note factory on DeepBook Predict. Describe a market view in plain English — "BTC above $66k by Friday, pays $100" — and Predict Studio builds a fairly-priced,   defined-risk note whose maximum loss is capped at the premium and enforced on-chain in the same transaction.

It turns DeepBook Predict's raw binary and range markets into retail-legible options: it repairs unsafe volatility surfaces, solves a gas-bounded sparse replication across Predict legs, and   mints a single owned StructuredPosition receipt.    On-chain options have stalled near $100M TVL because building a payoff — picking strikes, legs, and sizes — is something normal users won't do.

Predict Studio collapses that into one sentence,   making spreads a UX problem instead of an infrastructure one.

About:

### Strata: Capital-Efficient Structured Options Vaults on Sui

### The Graveyard of Billion-Dollar Ideas

Historically, DeFi options vaults like Ribbon Finance and Friktion raised serious capital and crossed billions in TVL by offering an elegant concept: deposit USDC, earn yield. However, they ultimately failed because their options were mispriced. When Bitcoin crashed 70% in 2022, their liquidity providers (LPs) were caught on the wrong side of every settlement. The idea was right, but the execution was fatally flawed by static volatility pricing.

Strata brings structured options vaults to Sui and fixes this core market failure. By building on DeepBook Predict, Strata prices options using a live Black-Scholes volatility surface derived from real market activity. No more selling volatility cheap, and no more blindsided LPs.

### Real-World Validation & Backtesting

Before deploying a single dollar of real capital, we backtested our core strategy against **six historical Bitcoin periods** to prove its structural advantage during black-swan events.

- **The Terra/Luna Collapse:** Over a 10-day period where BTC dropped 35%, an unhedged Predict LP would have lost 34.7%. Because our hedge successfully fired and paid out, Strata’s hedged loss was contained to just 11.2%.

- **The 2022 Bear Market:** During a sustained 65% drawdown, Strata successfully capped the downside and protected LP capital from severe depletion.

### The Technology: Live on Sui Testnet

Strata operates two live smart-contract strategies on the Sui testnet, fully integrated with DeepBook Predict:

- **STRATA-PH (PLP + Hedge):** 90% of deposits supply Predict's liquidity pool, earning a premium as binary options settle every 15 minutes. The remaining 10% buys an out-of-the-money binary put—crash insurance that is automatically evaluated and redeemed by a permissionless keeper during every oracle period.

- **STRATA-RL (Range Ladder):** This strategy places five vertical range positions around the current Bitcoin price. It is entirely direction-neutral, paying out wherever BTC lands within the ladder on settlement, making it perfect for LPs with no directional view.

**Meaningful Composability:** Both strategies mint a `VAULT_SHARE` to depositors. This isn't a simple receipt token—it is a standard Sui Coin. It is fully composable, meaning it can be stacked as collateral in margin protocols or used in other DeFi primitives within the exact same Programmable Transaction Block (PTB).

**The First On-Chain Volatility Dashboard:** We also built the first realized volatility benchmark on the Sui network. We compute 7-day and 30-day annualized realized volatility from Predict's oracle settlement history (over 4,700 data points) and compare it against the current implied volatility from Predict's live prices.

*(For the technical judges: Our Move Package ID on the Sui testnet is *`0xc579b44c2070110f5e4690e7177c041d05e44f1b65e5a78ca93ab974843df9a9`*, utilizing the core factory function *`strata::vault_factory::register_vault`*)*

### A Next-Generation User Experience

We turned vault creation into a conversation, and vault discovery into a marketplace.

- **AI Vault Creator:** Our vault factory is open to anyone. You simply describe your strategy in plain English via a chat interface. GPT-4o-mini extracts the exact parameters (hedge ratio, strike offset, risk level), and you can deploy the configuration on-chain with a single click.

- **Community Marketplace:** Community vaults are instantly listed in a public `/vaults` marketplace. The creator earns a permanent 10 basis point (bps) fee on every deposit made into their vault. We already have five community vaults registered.

### Built for Reliability: Unsimulated Stress Testing

We didn't just build a demo; we built reliable infrastructure. We ran a 10-day continuous integration (CI) bot to stress-test the full vault lifecycle on the live testnet. Executing every 15 minutes, the bot ran for 184 cycles and completed 57 verified on-chain transactions across 5 different LP wallets.

**Live Bug Resolution:** During this 184-cycle run, we actually caught a real share supply inflation bug. By querying on-chain `Deposited` events, we traced the share ratio drifting from 1:1 toward 100,000,000:1 over 40 cycles. We successfully identified the root cause (deposit window mismanagement around PLP deployments), fixed the bot, and republished a clean package. Every transaction is verifiable on Suiscan.

### The Long-Term Vision

Strata provides the missing, retail-friendly application layer on top of DeepBook Predict's complex infrastructure. By allowing users to deposit capital without managing Greeks or executing order books manually, Strata acts as a primary onboarding funnel for DeFi TVL into the Sui ecosystem.

Moving forward, Strata will expand beyond DeepBook binaries into delta-neutral basis trading, yield aggregation, and automated treasury management—proving that complex financial engineering can exist alongside a consumer-grade user experience.

**1. Started on testnet.** DeepBook Predict lives only on Sui testnet today, so the testnet bot mints, settles, and redeems binary positions through real Move calls (`predict::mint`, `redeem_permissionless`, `predict_manager::*`) with faucet dUSDC — on-chain proof of the full integration. Visible as the *testnet* option in the dashboard's network toggle.

Validated under real money on Polymarket.** Every 15s the mainnet bot prices BTC binaries against Predict's continuous **SVI surface**, compares to Polymarket's discrete-strike order books, and trades the mispriced side with real `pUSD` on **Polygon mainnet**. The day Predict ships on Sui mainnet, the Sui mint flips with a single config change.

**3. Made it delta-neutral with a Hyperliquid hedge.** Every Polymarket fill opens a sized BTC perp on **Hyperliquid mainnet** in the opposite direction, stripping directional exposure to leave *pure-vol PnL*. On UMA resolution the bot auto-redeems via the NegRiskAdapter and closes the hedge with a reduce-only IOC. Lives on `/poly-arb`.

**4. Added an IV-RV divergence strategy.** Standalone HL ticker — trades when Predict's implied vol diverges from realized vol, independent of the cross-venue arb. `/vol-arb`.

**5. Built the three-protocol composition.** A paper-mode **Margin-Lever** strategy constructs real PTBs against `predict` × `deepbook_margin` × `iron_bank` — the on-chain story the brief calls out by name. Signed flow gated on USDsui collateral funding. `/margin-lever`.

**Single-operator by design** — *no users, no pooled funds, no securities-law exposure*. The dashboard ships a Gatheral **SVI arbitrage-free checker** on `/surface`, a **calibration scatter** proving entry edge predicts realized return, signal-by-signal audit trail, and on-chain wallet truth — 174 tests behind it.

*"The single most realistic mainnet-day-one strategy — and it doubles as a live stress test of the SVI feeder."* — DeepBook Predict, Idea #7. **SVX is that bot.**

About:

A dUSDC-native capital system that converts any Sui asset into structured volatility and staking yield across DeepBook Predict, Margin, and Iron Bank.

### **The problem**

The majority of SUI's supply is staked and stops at a base APY around 2.5%, with no structured layer built on top of it. Stablecoin and BTC holders have no route into volatility yield without running a trading desk themselves. And DeepBook Predict, a real market with a live, tradeable volatility surface has no product yet that routes ordinary capital into it. Sui DeFi TVL has grown into the billions, almost all of it earning base rates, while the infrastructure to deploy it intelligently doesn't exist.

### **The solution**

Reflux accepts the assets people actually hold — native SUI, liquid-staked tokens (vSUI, afSUI, haSUI), restaked tokens, Scallop-lent assets, ordinary USDC from any venue, and BTC, and normalizes everything into a single dUSDC-denominated NAV. A volatility-adaptive allocation engine then deploys that NAV across DeepBook Predict (PLP supply and range strips), DeepBook Margin (leverage against staked collateral), DeepBook Spot (asset conversion at deposit and exit), and an internal Iron Bank credit layer (idle-capital parking and an instant-withdrawal buffer). In return, depositors hold rfUSD a single transferable, composable share token whose NAV compounds as the engine works.

The whole system is divided into three layers:

**what comes in** (any Sui asset), **what runs underneath** (a dUSDC-native allocation engine that explains every decision it makes), and **what comes out** (rfUSD, exitable instantly under the buffer cap).

### **What makes the allocation engine different**

Most yield vaults hardcode a fixed split between strategies. Reflux's allocator reads the live SVI implied-volatility surface from the DeepBook Predict indexer and shifts capital accordingly toward range strips when premia is rich relative to crash risk, toward PLP and idle Iron Bank yield when implied vol is thin. Every reallocation emits an on-chain event recording the exact trigger (ATM IV vs. threshold), the regime, and the before/after weights, surfaced in plain language on a public risk dashboard that needs no wallet to view. It's built to be a transparent, auditable autonomous fund rather than a black box, every claim of intelligence is paired with a claim of proof.

The Iron Bank layer also solves the worst UX failure mode of fixed-allocation vaults: locked exits. Withdrawals under the buffer cap settle instantly, funded by parked idle capital, and the buffer is automatically repaid from Predict settlements at the next keeper roll, so users can exit even while every other dollar is deployed in live positions.

### **Technical architecture**

Reflux is a suite of Move contracts on Sui composing five protocols in atomic programmable transaction blocks: liquid-staking adapters, DeepBook Spot for USDC/BTC↔dUSDC conversion, DeepBook Margin for leverage, DeepBook Predict for premia and PLP yield, and an internal Iron Bank credit module. A keeper rolls positions on every sub-hour expiry; settling, repaying the buffer, checking risk, and redeploying based on the allocator's read of the current vol regime. Safety is trustless by design: emergency deleverage can be triggered by anyone when LTV is breached, and hard caps on exposure and buffer draw cannot be exceeded even by an admin. The strategy has been verified against 400+ replayed historical expiries, simulating a 9.8–13.1% APY range with a 2.4% max drawdown and zero LTV breaches across the test set.

### **Traction**

The team previously won a Sui Overflow hackathon with Sui Wallet Bot, which evolved into Kiwi Protocol and has since processed over $5M in cross-chain transfer volume. Ahead of this submission, the team surveyed holders of every asset class Reflux accepts: SUI, liquid-staked tokens, restaked tokens, Scallop-lent assets, stablecoins, and BTC, among wallets holding over $1M on Sui. 62% responded, and 55% of respondents committed to deposit on Reflux immediately at mainnet launch, which is planned to follow directly after DeepBook Predict's own mainnet launch.

About:

**SurfaceVault **is the LP-side optimizer for DeepBook Predict: a dynamic, Greeks-aware liquidity layer that makes every Predict market more capital-efficient, not a competing product.

A static PLP position is short volatility across a moving surface. It bleeds vega it cannot see or hedge, and the EVM "DeFi Option Vault" sector collapsed around 90% from peak TVL on exactly this flaw. SurfaceVault fixes the LP side. A depositor deposits dUSDC and receives one vault token (svPLP), and a Nautilus TEE rebalancer reads the live Block Scholes SVI volatility surface and migrates PLP liquidity toward the minimum-variance, maximum-fee region every time the surface moves. Execution is a single keeper-signed programmable transaction guarded by a hot-potato RebalanceApproval, so a malicious keeper cannot rebalance to anything the enclave did not sign.

This is proven on-chain, not mocked. It is live on Sui testnet: a real enclave-attested rebalance deployed 80 dUSDC into the genuine Predict package in one atomic transaction (deploy_idle, then predict::supply three times, then execute_rebalance; transaction 4yMADqBR..., epoch 1137). The parity kill-shot: our enclave's fixed-point compute_nd2 reproduces the on-chain oracle::compute_nd2 byte for byte at 1e9, proven four ways (Move, Rust, Node, and live in the browser), with a difference of exactly zero. The 40 percent per-slot allocation cap is enforced in both the Rust optimizer and the Move execution.

Deep, sticky LP capital is how a new options venue survives, and SurfaceVault is how you get it.

About:

Aegis is an on-chain execution protection protocol that defends large trades against front-running and slippage on DeepBook V3. It accepts a trader's intent, scores live order-book risk across five signals, and deploys a Gaussian-weighted grid of child orders atomically in a single Sui Programmable Transaction Block :- all-or-nothing, with no exposed footprint.

🌐 Live Application: [https://aegis-self-defending-deepbook-execu-gray.vercel.app/](https://aegis-self-defending-deepbook-execu-gray.vercel.app/)

📦 Package ID: `0xd15c4493777c5e7b0bf653d47278d386fee73e235a2a59fae335b44ce2dc8a0b`

🔗 Live TX: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/7HZJLybFFThjKShL6cwBzqmYz4YdY94B1Gs3isHGe6Ln](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/7HZJLybFFThjKShL6cwBzqmYz4YdY94B1Gs3isHGe6Ln)

📄 Whitepaper: [https://github.com/savageyoongi/Aegis-Self-defending-Deepbook-execution/blob/main/docs/whitepaper.md](https://github.com/savageyoongi/Aegis-Self-defending-Deepbook-execution/blob/main/docs/whitepaper.md)

**Inspiration**

On-chain order books are transparent by design and that transparency is a weapon in the wrong hands. Submit a large market buy on DeepBook and every bot on the network sees your intent before your transaction lands. They front-run the ask side, widen the spread, and pocket the difference. The problem is structural: DeepBook gives you raw order placement but no protection layer. There is no way to express "fill me across a price band, atomically, without telegraphing my size." A single large order is a target. Aegis makes it disappear into a defended grid.

**The Fix**

- Aegis replaces single large orders with a Gaussian-weighted grid of child limit orders spread across a dynamic price band. The attacker never sees the full size at a single price.

-

- A five-signal risk engine scores the live order book - spread, volatility, bid-ask imbalance, depth thinness and market footprint, producing a single score between 0 and 1. A hostile book gets more slices spread wider; a calm book gets fewer slices tighter to mid.

-

- Every child order is assembled into one Sui Programmable Transaction Block. Because DeepBook aborts the entire transaction on any single order failure, the grid is atomically all-or-nothing. Either every slice lands or none do. There is no partial fill an adversary can exploit.

-

- Collateral is managed through a BalanceManager — DeepBook V3's on-chain collateral object — so funds are locked at the protocol level, never in a hot wallet.

-

- A built-in predator simulator models adversarial front-running against both a naive market order and the Aegis grid, making slippage savings visible and measurable in real time. In a toxic market with 750 SUI, Aegis cuts slippage from 244 bps to 78 bps — a 166 bps saving per trade.

**Progress During Hackathon**

Aegis was built entirely from scratch during the hackathon. We started from the math, a Gaussian-weighted order distribution and worked outward to the chain. The riskiest piece was the DeepBook V3 SDK integration. The `@mysten/deepbook-v3` package nests its call path differently from the documentation, requires a gRPC client for transaction building due to `resolveTransactionPlugin`, and then hangs on gRPC execution. We built a hybrid: gRPC for building, HTTP JSON-RPC for submission.

The pool constraints had to be discovered empirically. The SUI/DBUSDC testnet pool enforces a 1 SUI lot size and 1 SUI minimum per order. BID orders lock DBUSDC while ASK orders lock SUI, which cost us two failed transactions before the collateral direction clicked. We implemented `snapToLot()` to round fractional planner quantities to valid on-chain multiples, and added `INTENT_MAX_SLICES` to cap order count against the available BalanceManager balance.

By the end we had a full vertical slice: a working risk engine, an adaptive Gaussian planner, a DeepBook V3 PTB executor placing real limit orders on testnet, a live Express API deployed on Railway, a React frontend on Vercel, and a published Sui Move package for on-chain intent traceability. The live transaction `7HZJLybFFThjKShL6cwBzqmYz4YdY94B1Gs3isHGe6Ln` confirms an `OrderPlaced` event with `placed_quantity: 1000000000` (1 SUI) settled atomically through a single PTB on DeepBook V3 testnet.

**Tech Stack**

Sui · Move · DeepBook V3 · JavaScript · Node.js · @mysten/sui gRPC · React · Railway · Vercel

**Fundraising Status**

Currently bootstrapped and actively seeking grants or pre-seed opportunities to extend Aegis to mainnet, integrate live order book feeds, and build the cancel-and-refresh cadence that turns the one-shot grid into a persistent execution engine.

About:

**Pred - Leveraged Prediction Markets**

Ever wondered why Polymarket, Kalshi, and every other big prediction market skipped leverage?

It's not that nobody wanted it.

It's that leverage breaks on binary resolution. A yes-or-no market can snap to zero in an instant. You can't lend safely against something that vanishes between one block and the next.

So everyone left leverage off the table. DeepBook Predict changes the math. It works differently. You provide the input. The oracle returns the odds.

That one shift cracks the problem open. And it made us wonder. Can we finally bring leverage to prediction markets?

That's Pred. Bet on prediction markets up to 5x.

**How it works**

Pick a market. Pick a side. Pick your leverage. Pred lends you the rest. A small stake controls a much bigger position.

If you're right, your profit multiplies. If the odds turn against you too far, your position closes early. You never owe more than you put in. Your downside is capped at your stake.

Want a 15 minute Bitcoin market? Set your strike price. Choose above or below. Set your bet and leverage. You're in.

Wait for it to resolve, or close early whenever you want.

There's a whole board of markets. Different assets. Different expiry dates. Fast 15 minute rounds and longer plays.

**Authentication & Deposit & Withdraw**

To bet, just deposit SUI or USDC.

DeepBook Predict settles in DUSDC, which is awkward to get. So Pred takes the currency you already hold and handles the swap in the background. You never think about it.

Connect a Slush wallet. Or sign in with zkLogin and skip the wallet entirely.

**Economy**

Here's the part that makes it work. Pred doesn't bet against you.

People betting up and people betting down cover each other. Pred takes a small fee in the middle.

When one side gets too heavy, Pred shifts its prices to pull the other side back.

And before a market resolves, Pred closes out the borrowed money. So nobody gets caught by the sudden jump at the end.

That single move is what the big platforms never solved.

**LP Pools**

The lending money isn't Pred's alone.

Anyone can stake into Pred's pool and back the house. The pool funds the leverage. In return, stakers earn a share of every fee Pred collects.

Idle capital doesn't sit still either. The spare part earns extra yield in DeepBook Margin in the background.

And the more people stake, the more leverage Pred can offer. That sends more fees back to stakers. It grows on itself.

**Links**

Test it by yourself: [Pred](https://sui.pred.now)

[Developer Documentation](https://docs.pred.now)

[Official X Account](https://x.com/preddotnow)

About:

**Echo — The First Sui Native Social Copy-Trading on DeepBook Predict**

**One-Line Pitch**

Echo is the first on-chain social copy-trading protocol built on DeepBook Predict — where followers copy top predictors in one transaction, payout splits are enforced by Move smart contract, and predictor reputation is computed entirely from settled on-chain outcomes.

---

**The Problem**

Copy trading is a $100B+ industry on centralized exchanges. Binance Copy Trade has 5M+ monthly active followers. eToro has 35M+ registered users. The demand is proven — retail traders want to follow experts, and experts want to monetize their edge. But the entire infrastructure runs on centralized rails with three fundamental problems.

1. Users Don't Know Who To Trust

There is no native, verified leaderboard on any on-chain prediction market. Win rates on eToro and Binance are computed by the platform using the platform's own methodology — not verifiable on-chain. Independent research found eToro's top performers showed 71% win rates on their profiles while copy-followers experienced 54% median returns on the same signals — a 17-point gap the platform controls entirely. On Polymarket, which does $25B+ monthly volume, there is no trader profile at all. Users fall back on Twitter threads and Telegram groups to find good traders. A whale can publicly call UP while holding DOWN on-chain with zero proof of intent either way.

2. Execution Is Manual and Painful

Following a trader today means monitoring their wallet manually, opening a separate position yourself, managing timing, and tracking outcomes by hand. Prediction markets move in minutes — by the time a user spots a trade and replicates it, the edge is gone. No on-chain prediction market platform has a native copy mechanism. Polymarket has $25B+ in monthly volume and zero built-in copy or follow features.

3. No Incentive for Good Traders to Share

Skilled traders have no revenue model for sharing their edge on-chain. If you post your position publicly, others free-ride without compensating you. On-chain analysts on Polymarket and Kalshi regularly build large social followings but no platform pays them for their alpha or lets followers copy them in-app. The value leaks entirely off-chain. The result: experts stay private, beginners stay blind, and the social layer never develops.

---

**Market Context**

---

**The Solution — Echo**

Echo is the social coordination layer built directly on top of DeepBook Predict. It turns an individual binary options interface into a two-sided marketplace:

- Predictors post trades on DeepBook through Echo, build an on-chain reputation from settled outcomes, and earn 15% of every follower's winning payout automatically

- Followers browse a live feed of verified predictors with on-chain win rates and streaks, copy any trade in one transaction, and receive 85% of their payout at settlement — split enforced by Move, not by trust

- Premium signals let predictors encrypt their direction and reasoning with SEAL, creating a paywall with cryptographic proof they were actually in the trade

**The full flow:**

1. Predictor posts trade → predict::mint opens a real DeepBook Predict position first

2. Optionally encrypts reasoning with SEAL → uploads to Walrus → creates SignalPolicy on-chain

3. Follower browses feed → sees predictor stats (win rate, streak, followers) from on-chain PredictorProfile

4. Follower optionally pays signal fee → SEAL decrypts direction + reasoning locally in browser

5. Follower copies → one PTB atomically deposits dUSDC + mints position on DeepBook + writes CopyRecord on-chain

6. At settlement → predict::redeem_permissionless settles → copy_trade::settle_copy enforces 85/15 split in the same PTB → PredictorProfile stats updated

---

**What Makes Echo Different**

The critical differentiator: the predictor's 15% is not a fee you send to a wallet you trust. It is a constant in copy_trade.move — const PREDICTOR_BPS: u64 = 1500 — enforced inside settle_copy, transferred atomically in the same PTB as the follower's 85%. There is no admin key, no upgrade authority on the payout logic, no configuration. The contract enforces it or the entire PTB reverts.

---

**DeepBook Integration**

Echo is built on top of DeepBook Predict, not alongside it. Every trade visible in Echo's feed is a real DeepBook Predict position. Echo adds the social coordination layer; DeepBook provides the binary option infrastructure, the SVI volatility model, the oracle, and the settlement mechanism.

Trade Execution

When a follower copies a trade, Echo builds a single Sui PTB:

- predict_manager::deposit — locks dUSDC into follower's PredictManager

- predict::mint — opens a binary option on DeepBook Predict with the exact same MarketKey as the predictor's trade

- copy_trade::create_copy — writes the CopyRecord on-chain linking follower to predictor

All three are atomic. If DeepBook rejects the mint (expired market, invalid key, insufficient balance), the copy record is never written and funds revert.

Market Key Construction

Every DeepBook Predict position is identified by: market_key::new(oracle_id, expiry_ms, strike_price, is_up: bool). Echo reads live oracle data from the DeepBook Predict Server REST API to construct valid keys and display SVI-implied probabilities in the feed.

Settlement

DeepBook's redeem_permissionless is non-custodial and oracle-final. Echo calls it first, then splits:

- predict::redeem_permissionless — oracle price settles the position, payout goes into PredictManager

- predict_manager::withdraw — extracts the Coin

- copy_trade::settle_copy — enforces 85/15 split, updates PredictorProfile stats, emits CopySettled event

Echo cannot influence the settlement price. The binary outcome is determined by the oracle, not by Echo.

**Live Feed**

Echo's social feed queries /positions/minted from the DeepBook Predict Server and filters to wallets with a registered PredictorProfile. The leaderboard, portfolio dashboard, and trade cards all read directly from DeepBook's indexer. Echo runs no off-chain database.

---

**SEAL Integration**

SEAL (Secure Encryption with Access Locks) is Sui's native threshold encryption protocol. Echo uses it to create a cryptographic link between a premium signal and the on-chain trade it describes.

The problem it solves: Signal providers on Telegram charge $99–$299/month with no proof they were ever in the trade they recommended. On Echo, a predictor cannot create a SEAL signal for a trade they did not open on DeepBook first.

**How it works:**

1. Predictor writes { direction, reasoning } → encrypted client-side with @mysten/seal SDK

2. Ciphertext uploaded to Walrus → blobId returned

3. seal_signal::create_policy(blob_id, fee_dusd) creates a SignalPolicy shared object on-chain tied to a specific oracle_id + expiry + strike

4. Follower calls seal_signal::pay_signal_fee(policy, payment) → wallet added to paid_wallets: VecSet<address>

5. SEAL key server calls seal_approve on-chain → verifies sender is in paid_wallets for that exact policy

6. Decryption key released → follower decrypts the blob locally in browser — key never touches a server

The SignalPolicy object stores the blobId, the fee in dUSDC, and the set of wallets that have paid. seal_approve is the on-chain gate the key server checks before releasing the key. No position, no policy, nothing to sell.

---

**Walrus Integration**

All trade reasoning — SEAL-encrypted premium signals and free plain-text reasoning — is stored on Walrus, not a centralized server.

- Encrypted blobs: { iv, ciphertext } from SEAL encrypt, uploaded via PUT /v1/blobs

- Plain-text signals: { direction, reasoning } JSON, uploaded directly

- Retrieval: GET /v1/blobs/:blobId — content-addressed, permanent for the epoch

- Native integration: Walrus is the only blob store where SEAL's key-release flow works natively on Sui — the key server verifies the access condition and fetches the blob on the same network

---

**Smart Contract Architecture**

Three Move modules deployed to Sui Testnet:

echo::predictor_profile

- ProfileRegistry — singleton shared object mapping address → PredictorProfile ID

- PredictorProfile — per-predictor on-chain stats: win_rate_bps, total_trades, winning_trades, current_streak, best_streak, copy_earnings_cents, signal_earnings_cents, follower_count

- Stats updated atomically at settlement inside settle_copy — no off-chain oracle, no trust, no self-reporting

echo::copy_trade

- CopyRecord — shared object linking follower → predictor with trade parameters, settlement outcome, and payout breakdown

- create_copy — verifies follower ≠ predictor, minimum amount, records the link

- settle_copy — enforces PREDICTOR_BPS = 1500 (15%), updates PredictorProfile, emits CopySettled event. The split is a constant, not a parameter.

echo::seal_signal

- SignalPolicy — shared object with blob_id, fee_dusd, paid_wallets: VecSet<address>

- seal_approve — entry function called by SEAL key server; verifies tx_sender is in paid_wallets before releasing decryption key

- pay_signal_fee — pays fee, adds wallet to paid_wallets, credits signal_earnings_cents to predictor profile

---

**What's Novel**

1. First social copy-trading layer on any on-chain prediction market — the category does not exist yet. Copy trading on-chain is an open problem.

2. 85/15 payout split enforced by Move — not an agreement, not a promise, a constraint in the contract. PREDICTOR_BPS is a constant. The split happens or the PTB reverts.

3. SEAL-gated premium signals with proof-of-trade — the predictor must have skin in the game. The SignalPolicy is tied to a real on-chain position. No position, no policy.

4. On-chain verifiable track record — [PredictorProfile.win](http://PredictorProfile.win)_rate_bps is computed from settled CopyRecords, written by the same PTB that transfers the money. Nothing is self-reported.

5. Atomic one-transaction copy — deposit + mint + CopyRecord in a single Sui PTB. Partial execution reverts automatically. No front-running gap between the predictor's trade and the follower's copy.

6. Direction hiding with wallet-based enforcement — trade direction is hidden from all non-posters via on-chain wallet check (isOwn). No localStorage, no cookies, no session state — if your wallet isn't the trader's wallet, you see a lock.

---

About:

**Moving a loan to a cheaper lender on Sui normally needs capital you do not have, and several separate transactions. RefiRail does it in one signature, with none of your own money.**

### **Live Now**

- **Live app:** [https://refirail.vercel.app](https://refirail.vercel.app), on Sui mainnet.

- **Open source:** [https://github.com/dmustapha/refirail](https://github.com/dmustapha/refirail)

- **Proven on mainnet:** real atomic transactions, each verifiable on Suiscan (deleverage, refinance Navi to Suilend, refinance Navi to AlphaLend, and a partial refinance).

- **Zero net-new Move:** composition-only over already-audited protocols, so it adds no new attack surface.

### **The Problem**

A borrower on Sui who is stuck at a high rate cannot easily move their loan. The unwind is repay the debt, free the collateral, redeposit somewhere cheaper, and borrow all over again. That needs **capital you do not have** while your collateral is still locked, across **several separately signed transactions**. If one step fails halfway through, you are left under-collateralized.

### **The Solution**

RefiRail collapses that whole chore into one atomic transaction. It does two things: pay down risk (deleverage), or move your loan to the cheapest money market (refinance). The key technical claim: a **fee-free DeepBook flash loan front-runs the entire sequence, so there is zero upfront capital**, and the whole Programmable Transaction Block reverts if it would ever leave you worse off. Every operation runs as a server-side dry-run against live mainnet first, so the app only ever hands your wallet a transaction it has already proven will not abort.

### **How It Works**

- **Detect.** Reads your real position across Navi, Suilend, and AlphaLend, straight off mainnet.

- **Route.** The best-rate router reads the live borrow APR at Suilend and AlphaLend and picks the cheaper venue, or you pick manually.

- **Preview.** A live mainnet dry-run shows the new rate and the new health factor, with no signature.

- **Flash and swap on DeepBook.** A fee-free DeepBook flash borrow of USDC opens the sequence; the swap routes `SUI to DEEP to USDC` for zero fee.

- **One atomic PTB.** Repay Navi, move the collateral, borrow at the new lender, repay the flash, all in a single signature. If any step fails, the whole thing reverts and nothing moves.

### **Key Features**

- **Zero upfront capital:** every operation opens with a fee-free DeepBook flash loan.

- **Fee-free two-hop swap:** `SUI to DEEP to USDC` through DeepBook whitelisted DEEP pairs (0 DEEP fee), versus a direct swap that would charge a DEEP taker fee the wallet does not hold.

- **Best-rate router:** reads the live borrow APR at Suilend and AlphaLend, routes to the cheaper, with a manual override.

- **Atomic or nothing:** the whole move is one reverting PTB, so a failure mid-way leaves your position untouched.

- **Honest previews:** every on-screen number is a live mainnet dry-run, no fabricated values, no signature until the transaction is proven safe.

- **Zero net-new Move:** composition-only over audited protocols, which is itself the technical thesis (no new attack surface).

### **Architecture**

`borrower |
   v
RefiRail  (one Programmable Transaction Block)
   |-- DeepBook v3: fee-free flash loan + fee-free SUI to DEEP to USDC swap
   |-- Navi:        repay debt + withdraw collateral (source)
   |-- Suilend / AlphaLend: deposit collateral + borrow at the lower rate (destination)
   |-- Pyth:        in-PTB oracle refresh
   v
atomic settle  (reverts if any leg fails)
`
### **Composed Protocols And Proof (Sui mainnet)**

RefiRail deploys zero custom Move. It is built entirely at the PTB layer over these live packages:

- **DeepBook v3 package:** `0x0e735f8c93a95722efd73521aca7a7652c0bb71ed1daf41b26dfd7d1ff71f748`

- **DeepBook SUI_USDC pool:** `0xe05dafb5133bcffb8d59f4e12465dc0e9faeaa05e3e342a08fe135800e3e4407`

- **Suilend Main Pool market:** `0x84030d26d85eaa7035084a057f2f11f701b7e2e4eda87551becbc7c97505ece1`

- **AlphaLend package:** `0xe48b33ef41d56e04fc42bf558e4d54d7cae8a363da9054a6c24bafc2c53a4f33`

- **Native USDC:** `0xdba34672e30cb065b1f93e3ab55318768fd6fef66c15942c9f7cb846e2f900e7::usdc::USDC`

Real on-chain proof (open on suiscan.xyz/mainnet/tx/):

- **Atomic deleverage (DeepBook flash + fee-free two-hop):** `4S5bhsgZhsrwjaavUNBAZKyDwWKxKfruUTUXD6jT3S8K`

- **Atomic refinance, Navi to Suilend:** `BiMBPK7sLPc1F4DNv4GRseCoLVWPb2oxNdR33Ep8wdsK`

- **Atomic refinance, Navi to AlphaLend (the multi-lender router):** `3UgVGY2ydYTRsQFAV7MFpxFe9frnFgJVFPhxbyFKuvL6`

- **Atomic partial refinance, 30% Navi to Suilend:** `8djvtzLpTa36oLUYs2jWGzTsExRd7HQdDb46WBUbF3WF`

About:

Metador is a non-custodial, tokenized-share vault for DeepBook Predict on Sui — PLP yield, with the crash hedged.

The problem: vaults and copy-trading today force a bad trade-off — hand custody to an operator, or trust a leader not to "drift" and do whatever they want with your deposit. The risk isn't bad actors; it's that you have to trust.

Metador removes the trust. Your capital stays in a vault you own. The leader holds a locked PredictTradeCap that can only act inside four on-chain walls, enforced by Move smart contracts — not by us:

- Scope — the vault can trade one pool only.

- Budget — a hard, immutable spend ceiling.

- Expiry — the cap auto-expires; trading stops.

- Revocable — one click, instant, irreversible.

DeepBook Predict owns pricing, the SVI vol surface, matching, settlement, and liquidation. Metador owns the vault, the policy walls, the tokenized share, the keeper, and the risk presentation — so an outside LP gets auditable, composable Predict exposure without the naked left-tail and without trusting an operator. Funds can't be stolen; losses are capped by your ceiling. No earnings are promised.

Built on Sui testnet: the full keel_core Move package (vault, four-walls policy, PLP + hedge strategy, shares/NAV, revoke) with abort-path unit tests — plus the complete product: vault marketplace, Network analytics, vault detail with a live policy card + budget meter + activity feed, a create-vault wizard with live policy preview, a one-click theatrical REVOKE, an SVI vol-surface viewer, and a PLP risk panel.

About:

**StructX **turns a view on prediction markets into a clear, defined-risk payoff built on **DeepBook Predic**t. A user can describe what they expect in plain language or choose a strategy directly. StructX then reads live binary and range markets, selects suitable strikes, sizes each position according to the user's budget and risk preference, and combines the legs into one complete payoff preview. Before committing any funds, the user can see the total cost, maximum loss, position structure, and possible result across different settlement prices.

Prediction markets are powerful financial building blocks, but creating a useful multi-leg position still requires market discovery, pricing, sizing, and careful transaction construction. Fulfilling the same intent manually means opening several DeepBook positions, configuring each one, dividing the budget across them, comparing live premiums, and reasoning about how every leg will behave at expiry. **StructX **brings that work into** one guided flow**. Once the user is satisfied with the payoff, funding and every position are created together in a single atomic Sui transaction approved from their own wallet.

The product is supported by a deterministic Rust compiler, live requoting, slippage protection, transaction simulation, and chain-verified execution records. This keeps the experience simple for the user while making the strategy transparent and reliable from preview to settlement. We see **StructX **as the foundation for a broader onchain structured products platform where people can express a market view, understand the risk they are taking, and keep full control of their funds.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/12c11c07-7e1f-41d9-bdf7-8787be77646a/9b175f9b-c4c7-4a21-990b-f2b86bc09667.png

About:

**Keel turns DeepBook Predict into an insurance rail.** It takes a product millions of crypto holders already want — “protect me if the market crashes” — and compiles it, under the hood, into a basket of DeepBook prediction-market positions. The user never sees a bet. They see a premium, a trigger price, and an automatic payout. Every dollar of that flow lands in DeepBook’s order books.

**The core idea (in one breath)**

A DeepBook binary market that pays **$1 if “BTC below $60,000”** is, economically, a **digital put option** — money arrives exactly when an asset falls past a level. That’s the same shape as an insurance payout.

***One binary is a digital put. A ladder of binaries is a real insurance policy.***

Stack a series of “below-$X” DeepBook markets at ascending strikes, give each a slice of the coverage, and the combined payout stops looking like a step and becomes a smooth, proportional loss curve:

`payout(p) = sumInsured × (T − p) / (T − F), clamped to [0, sumInsured]`

So Keel doesn’t build a new insurance protocol with its own pool, adjusters, and claims process. **It expresses insurance as a portfolio of DeepBook positions** and lets DeepBook’s liquid, permissionless market do the pricing and the settlement.

**Why this matters for DeepBook**

Prediction markets today are used almost entirely by speculators. That’s a ceiling on who shows up and why. Keel is built to break through it:

- **A reason to participate that isn’t gambling.** “Don’t let a crash wipe me out” is a need ordinary holders already have. Keel turns that need into DeepBook order flow — from users who would never place a bet but will happily buy insurance.

- **The market mechanics are completely hidden.** Buyers think in dollars, triggers, and payouts. Keel’s indemnity engine compiles that down to strikes, legs, and DeepBook orders for them, so the non-degen audience can actually onboard.

- **Organic, utility-driven flow.** These orders exist because someone wants protection — not because they’re chasing a number. That’s exactly the demand that deepens liquidity and tightens spreads on DeepBook’s “below-$X” markets.

- **A two-sided flywheel, both sides net-new to DeepBook.** Cover-buyers create demand; underwriters supply capital to a DeepBook vault to earn the premiums; more capital means cheaper, deeper cover; cheaper cover pulls in more buyers.

**In short:** DeepBook gave us a permissionless engine for pricing uncertainty. Keel points that engine at a problem everyone understands and converts insurance demand into prediction-market liquidity — bringing bet-averse, crash-averse holders onto DeepBook without ever asking them to think like a trader.

**How a policy becomes DeepBook positions**

When you buy cover you choose three things:

- **Sum insured** — how much protection (e.g. $10,000)

- **Trigger T** — where protection starts (e.g. BTC $60,000)

- **Floor F** — where you’re fully paid out (e.g. BTC $51,000)

Keel’s indemnity engine snaps T and F onto DeepBook’s live oracle grid and splits the request into N “down-binary” legs — each one a DeepBook position that pays if settlement is below its strike. The strikes ascend evenly between F and T, each leg carrying an equal slice of the sum insured. As price falls through the band, one DeepBook rung after another pays out, tracking the linear loss curve.

**Worked example — $10,000 of BTC cover, T = $60,000, F = $51,000:**

- BTC settles at $62,000 — above trigger, no loss — payout $0

- BTC settles at $58,500 — 25% down the band — payout ~$2,500

- BTC settles at $55,500 — halfway down the band — payout ~$5,000

- BTC settles at $51,000 — at/below floor, max cover — payout $10,000

**No actuary sets the premium — the DeepBook order book does.** You pay what the market thinks the risk is worth.

**Who pays the claim? Underwriters on DeepBook.**

Every binary leg has two sides. Buyers take the “it crashes” side; **underwriters take the other side and collect the premium.** In Keel, underwriters supply capital to a DeepBook-backed vault that quotes and fills the binary ladders cover-buyers request.

- Market doesn’t crash → underwriters keep the premiums (a yield on capital, uncorrelated to lending rates or token emissions).

- Market does crash → their capital pays the cover, exactly as the DeepBook binaries settle on-chain.

Same risk/return as selling puts or running an insurance book — but transparent, collateralized, and permissionless. Anyone can be the insurance company.

**Automatic payouts — no claims, ever**

A small keeper service watches DeepBook for settled, in-the-money positions and calls `redeem_permissionless` so the payout lands in each policyholder’s own wallet without anyone filing anything. You don’t submit a claim. You don’t wait for an adjuster. The DeepBook market settles, the position pays, the money arrives.

**How it’s built**

- **apps/web** — Next.js 15 frontend + keel API routes (quote, buy, holdings, LP, stats).

- **apps/keeper** — long-running keeper that redeems settled in-the-money cover on DeepBook.

- **packages/shared** — ledger, Sui + DeepBook indexer clients, and the indemnity (ladder) engine.

- **packages/sui-bindings** — generated TypeScript bindings for the DeepBook Predict package.

Running live on Sui testnet — a new primitive: prediction-market-native insurance, built entirely on DeepBook.

About:

**SuiOptions — First Options Trading Protocol on Sui**

DeFi isn't really complete without options. They're one of the most basic tools in any serious market, used for hedging risk, generating yield, and speculating without putting up the full size of a position. Ethereum has had this covered for years through Lyra and Dopex, Solana has Zeta Markets. Sui has had nothing. If you hold SUI and want to hedge it or earn yield on it while you wait, your only option has been to leave the Sui ecosystem entirely.

SuiOptions was built to close that gap entirely.

It brings European-style call and put options on-chain, built directly on top of DeepBook Predict's orderbook instead of sitting beside it. Every option that gets written is a real Sui object, not an internal ledger entry somewhere off-chain. It can be held, transferred, or sold like anything else native to the chain. Settlement doesn't rely on the protocol's own judgment either, it's verified through Pyth Network's price oracle, so the price used to determine a payout is the same trustless feed the rest of DeFi already relies on.

The lifecycle of an option runs through four clear stages:

- **WRITE:** A creator locks SUI as collateral and mints either a CALL or PUT option object, choosing the strike price and expiry. The collateral stays locked on-chain until the option is exercised or expires.

- **LIST:** The option gets listed directly on DeepBook Predict's orderbook, where its price is discovered through real bid and ask activity, not set arbitrarily by the protocol.

- **EXERCISE:** Before expiry, a holder can exercise. The Move contract checks three things before releasing anything: is the option still active, is it before expiry, and is the live Pyth price actually in the money. Only if all three pass does the collateral move.

- **EXPIRE:** If the option finishes out of the money, the writer automatically reclaims their collateral. No manual cleanup, no separate transaction needed.

For people who'd rather not actively trade, there's a covered call vault sitting on top of all this. Deposit SUI, and the vault automatically writes 10% out-of-the-money calls every week, listing them on DeepBook the same way a manual writer would, and generates an estimated 3-8% weekly yield from the premiums. If a written call expires worthless, the collateral just comes back, no intervention required.

Every option, every market, and every settlement is verifiable on-chain. Nothing about pricing or payout depends on trusting the protocol's word for it, it depends on Pyth's oracle and DeepBook's orderbook, the same infrastructure the rest of Sui DeFi is built on.

SuiOptions is live on Sui testnet right now, with real markets listed on DeepBook, real Pyth-verified settlement, and a working vault you can actually deposit SUI into.

About:

**The Problem:** Providing liquidity on order book DEXs is notoriously complex and risky. For a user to run an automated market maker bot, they traditionally have to surrender their private keys to a server, exposing all their funds to massive security risks.

**The Solution** SurgeBot is a fully non-custodial, high-frequency AI market maker built exclusively for DeepBook V3 on the Sui blockchain. It utilises the Avellaneda-Stoikov mathematical model to adjust quotes dynamically based on real-time market volatility.

**How it works:**

- **Trustless Delegation (RiskProxy):** The AI agent is mathematically prevented from stealing your funds. DeepBook V3 requires an owned `TradeCap` to execute trades. SurgeBot locks that `TradeCap` inside a shared smart contract (`risk_proxy.move`) that only forwards execution commands if they pass hard-coded risk checks (like max drawdown and max position size).

- **Avellaneda-Stoikov Pricing Engine:** An off-chain TypeScript engine calculates the reservation price (fair value adjusted for inventory risk) and the optimal spread based on an EWMA volatility estimator.

- **Dual Yield Farming:** The architecture automatically stakes your DEEP tokens in DeepBook V3 to farm maker rebates and reduce taker fees while quoting.

- **Modern Dashboard:** A sleek React frontend built with `@mysten/dapp-kit` allows users to securely deploy their RiskProxy, deposit funds, and monitor live trading metrics.

By separating the **Agent** (which holds no funds) from the **Principal** (the user's wallet), SurgeBot enables anyone to run institutional-grade HFT strategies on Sui without compromising their private keys.

Strike brings the first **leveraged trading **platform for BTC **prediction markets** to Sui. It's built to overcome the capital inefficiency traders face on prediction markets like Polymarket and Kalshi. By providing leverage on SUI collateral, Strike aims to be the home for traders with less capital and high conviction.

Upon collateralizing SUI, the protocol triggers the Margin Loop: it programmatically borrows DBUSDC via DeepBook Margin, executes the necessary asset swaps, and opens a leveraged position on DeepBook Predict. Strike's Move contract orchestrates fund management and cross-protocol interactions, giving users a straightforward UX with a single transaction.

To mitigate systemic risk, an on-chain kinked interest rate model calculates a dynamic Health Factor for all active positions. Strike deploys a permissionless liquidation architecture, eliminating the need for centralized clearinghouses. External keeper bots can monitor on-chain state to liquidate undercollateralized positions, earning a 2% fee reward on a FIFO basis to ensure robust, decentralized protocol security.

About:

**🌌 Axis Predict: Autonomous DeFAI Option Vault on Sui**

*Capture premium yield autonomously on Sui using DeepBook Predict range options and AI-driven volatility strategies.*

🎯 Overview

**Axis Predict** is an autonomous, decentralized Delta-Neutral yield vault built on **Sui Testnet**. It acts as a passive yield harvester that pools user assets `dUSDC`) and delegates option writing strategies to a self-executing **AI Trading Agent**.

By utilizing** DeepBook Predict** (Sui's volatility-priced prediction/option market protocol), the Vault continuously writes range options. The AI Agent determines the optimal, safest price ranges by analyzing spot volatility and implied SVI curves, executing rolling strategy cycles autonomously to grow the Vault's Net Asset Value (NAV).

⚡ The Problem

Volatility harvesting and option writing on-chain are highly lucrative but present steep barriers for average users:

1. **Mathematical Complexity: **Pricing options using SVI (Stochastic Volatility Inspired) curves, understanding implied volatility, and setting optimal strike ranges require advanced quantitative finance knowledge.

2. **Active Management & Gas Wastage:** Option contracts have strict expiry durations. Manually monitoring, settling, and opening new positions leads to idle capital, human errors, and unnecessary write transaction gas fees.

3. **Risk Allocation: **Balancing risk limits (e.g., not exposing 100% of capital to a single trade) is hard to enforce consistently in manual execution.

💡 The Solution: Axis Predict

Axis Predict bridges the gap between complex prediction markets and retail users by introducing a DeFAI (Decentralized Finance + AI) Vault:

**For Users: **Simply deposit `dUSDC` and receive `pUSDC` vault shares. The vault grows passively as premiums are harvested.

**For the Protocol: **An on-chain shared vault delegates strategy execution rights via a `StrategyCap` to an AI Agent.

**For the Agent: **The Agent runs on a cost-optimized, expiry-driven scheduler. It checks blockchain states for free (read-only RPC calls), stands standby while options are active, and queries LLM models (GPT-4o-mini via OpenRouter) to determine the safest strike range *only* when the vault has idle cash to deploy.

🛠️ Key Features

**Move Smart Contracts:** Secure, decentralized Vault `AxisVault`) that handles pooling, issues liquidity share tokens `pUSDC`), and mathematically computes share value based on total assets.

**Expiry-Driven Automation Loop: **The backend agent scheduler is highly optimized:

**Free Polling:** Performs read-only RPC checks (zero gas cost).

**Standby: **Goes to sleep until the exact UNIX expiry timestamp of the active ticket.

**Autonomous Settle & Rebalance: **Settles expired contracts automatically and triggers the next cycle.

**Dynamic Capital Scaling:** Runs simulations `preview`) to auto-size trades to fit under configured budget caps and available liquidity.

**Live AI Reasoning Stream:** The frontend dashboard displays real-time execution logs from the backend agent (e.g., when the AI decides a range, when it executes, when it monitors, and when it settles).

**WIB Timezone Sync: **All dates and milestones on the interface are converted to Western Indonesian Time `Asia/Jakarta` / `UTC+7`) for visual alignment.

⚙️ How It Works (Technical Workflow)

1. **User Deposit: **Users deposit `dUSDC` into the `AxisVault` Move contract and receive `pUSDC` shares representing their portion of the vault's capital.

2. **State Monitoring: **The Node.js Backend Agent monitors the Vault's liquidity. If the Vault has idle funds, it initiates the AI Reasoning loop.

3. **AI Decision Engine: **The Agent queries the current Spot Price and SVI price curves from DeepBook Predict, packages the market state, and calls an LLM (GPT-4o-mini via OpenRouter). The AI decides if conditions are favorable and recommends the safest lower/higher strikes.

4. **On-Chain Execution: **The Agent uses the Vault's `StrategyCap` to call `allocate_range_position` on-chain, lock `dUSDC`, and mint a range option contract on DeepBook Predict.

5. **Standby: **The Agent sleeps until the option's expiry.

6. **Auto Settlement:** Upon expiration, the Agent executes `settle_range_position`. The payout (principal + earned premium) is returned to the Vault, increasing the share price of `pUSDC`.

💻 Tech Stack

**Smart Contracts: **Sui Move (Move 2024 edition)

**Backend: **Node.js, Express, TypeScript, `@mysten/sui` SDK

**Frontend:** Next.js, React, Tailwind CSS (or Custom CSS), Lucide React

**AI Model Engine: **GPT-4o-mini (orchestrated via OpenRouter API)

**DeFi Market Integrations:** DeepBook Predict (Range Options Market)

About:

**Insuirance** is an **on-chain parametric hedge cover protocol** built on Sui, powered by **DeepBook Predict**. It turns complex binary options into a product anyone can use: pick a drop threshold, pay a premium, hold a **Policy NFT**, and get paid out automatically if the price falls.

### **The Problem**

Crypto holders need hedging, but every existing tool fails them. **Centralized exchanges** impose KYC walls and custody risk. **On-chain perps** bleed funding costs and require constant management. **DeFi "insurance"** relies on human claims committees and governance votes that resolve days after the damage is done. None pay out automatically. None are truly trustless.

There is no simple way to say: *"If BTC drops 20% this week — pay me."* Insuirance is that answer.

### **How It Works**

- **Pick your trigger** — choose a drop % and expiry date. Strike price locks on-chain at purchase.

- **Pay the premium** — priced in real time by **DeepBook's SVI (Stochastic Volatility Inspired) model**. The premium is the only cost.

- **Hold the Policy NFT** — your cover is a native Sui object in your wallet, verifiable on-chain.

- **Auto-settle** — at expiry, the oracle confirms the price. Below strike → **dUSDC pays out automatically**. No claims. No humans. Just code.

### **Pricing Model**

Premium is priced as a **binary DOWN option** using the **SVI formula** (Gatheral, 2004) — the same parameterization used by institutional options desks, now fully on-chain via DeepBook Predict. `N(−d₂)` is the risk-neutral probability that BTC hits the strike at expiry. Deeper drops = lower premium = higher payout ratio.

### **Technical Highlights**

- **On-chain SVI params** fetched directly from the oracle object via Sui RPC — not the indexer — to prevent stale-param pricing errors that cause on-chain aborts

- `computeMaxViableBps` — binary-searches the maximum drop% where the on-chain `up_price < 1e9` invariant holds, preventing invalid transaction submissions

- `computeMaxUnclampedBps` — binary-searches the `min_strike` clamping threshold, so the UI never shows a slider range where the premium has already plateaued

- `redeem_permissionless` — expired positions settle without user action; payouts are delivered automatically

- **ShieldVault LP pool** — liquidity providers deposit dUSDC and earn premiums as the options seller side; the vault absorbs payout risk

### **Stack**

**Sui Move** · **DeepBook Predict** (binary option minting, SVI oracle, PLP vault) · **Next.js + Sui dApp Kit** · **GCP Cloud Run**

### **Why This Matters for DeepBook**

Insuirance is a **real-world application layer on DeepBook Predict** — it makes a raw derivative primitive accessible to non-traders as a simple risk management product. As DeepBook expands oracle coverage and tightens SVI parameters on mainnet, the range of viable drop thresholds grows with it. This is what DeepBook Predict is capable of building toward.

About:

**RollVault** is a structured DeepBook Predict vault: users deposit dUSDC and receive transferable RVLT share coins. A keeper runs an SVI-dynamic BTC range ladder (UP/DOWN strip around ATM each expiry), harvests maker spread, auto-rolls at settlement, and optionally splits NAV into senior (RVLT) and junior (JVLT) tranches. The prod dashboard at `https://rollvault-app.vercel.app` reads live VaultState from testnet RPC and renders NAV, range ladder, 3D vol surface, stress scenarios, Walrus NAV reports, deposit/withdraw flows, and Monte Carlo simulation results.

**Why it is built**

DeepBook Predict needs constant liquidity on both sides of every binary. PLP supply earns yield but issues no portable structured token. RollVault packages LP capital into a defined strike policy (SVI-derived band from on-chain OracleSVI), a keeper-driven roll primitive, transferable RVLT shares, and optional senior/junior waterfall tranches.

**Amazing features**

1. Senior / junior tranches (RVLT / JVLT)
TrancheVault (`0x6bc5533…`) splits NAV into senior RVLT (8% APR cap, principal protected up to balance) and junior JVLT (first-loss, excess upside). Waterfall proof on `ABA1Ed9w…`: senior_credit 21 micros, junior_credit 29,979 micros on a +20% cycle. Portable Sui Coins, not fairline-style non-portable claims.

2. 3D vol surface from live on-chain SVI inputs
Canvas mesh built from VaultState `k_sigma`, `sigma_period`, forward (or SVI_OPEN reference when idle). Deterministic sigma skew formula, not trained ML and not implied vol from market quotes. Same oracle params the keeper reads at `mark_positions_opened`.

3. Stress panel (honest client-side math)
Scenario rows for +/-1/2/3 sigma BTC moves with payout hit and senior/junior % change bars. Health score 0-100 with Healthy/Moderate/Stressed label. Formulas in `riskGate.ts` over real TrancheVault reads. Say explicitly: "Auditable formulas, not FairLine ML."

4. Walrus NAV anchors with re-hash match
Keeper uploads NAV report JSON per cycle; on-chain SHA256 recorded (`C5qhVFLA…`). Stress panel shows GREEN re-hash badge when blob bytes match committed digest. Blob `vstrWuKb…` fetchable from Walrus testnet aggregator (TTL caveat: re-anchor if 404).

5. Monte Carlo simulation (honest client-side)
SimResults panel: expected return, profitable runs %, per-cycle Sharpe, k_sigma 0.75. Output from `scripts/simulate.mjs` with footer `simulatedOn` date. Not backtest on realized Predict data. NavHistory chart is the honest on-chain counterpart (Rolled events, may look flat on thin testnet pools).

6. RVLT composable share coin
Depositors receive transferable `Coin<RVLT>` proportional to NAV (`vault::deposit`). Real object `0xaf1c1a49…`. Honest limit: no lending protocol integration demonstrated yet; composability is the design, DeFi wiring is future work.

7. SVI-dynamic range ladder + auto-roll (supporting proof)
Strikes from on-chain OracleSVI at OPEN, not hardcoded ticks. Seven-tx showcase cycle: two expiries, six PositionMinted, one Rolled event (`AJu9CpPC…`). Settlement loop uses real predict_manager withdraw coin, not keeper subsidy (`ArqKbffr…`, NAV +53,463 micros PnL).
