About:

Arena is a crowd consensus prediction market built for the DeepBook Predict track.

Traditional prediction markets usually trade external facts: prices, elections, sports results, or news outcomes. Arena focuses on a different and much higher-frequency category: how a clearly defined crowd will ultimately judge, choose, or accept a proposition. Examples include which AI model output human reviewers prefer, whether a DAO community supports a grant, whether core users prefer product concept A or B, or which side a fan/community audience will collectively choose.

Arena turns these crowd consensus outcomes into markets that can be created, participated in, verified, and settled.

For the DeepBook track, Arena uses DeepBook Predict as the on-chain trading and settlement layer for binary outcome markets. Each Arena proposition can become a DeepBook-powered binary market. Before reveal, users can trade or take a position while seeing only public progress, not the live answer direction. Meanwhile, Arena collects responses from the target crowd, performs basic quality checks, counts effective samples, and produces an official consensus result once the time and sample thresholds are met. After reveal, the market resolves around that verified consensus outcome.

This creates a new supply source for DeepBook markets: not only price-based or event-based predictions, but human consensus itself. Arena can support entertainment debates, brand research, AI evaluation, DAO governance, content moderation, and community validation, all with market incentives and transparent settlement.

The current MVP demonstrates a complete non-rolling, single-question, binary consensus market flow: market feed, proposition detail, participation experience, demo session, proposition creation, response tasks, result reveal, watchlist, account state, and a minimum settlement path. The DeepBook version focuses on mapping Arena’s consensus-result engine to DeepBook Predict’s binary position and settlement primitives, making crowd judgment outcomes tradable, liquid, and composable on Sui.

About:

Variance is one of the oldest traded primitives in finance—the swing in an asset's price, not its direction. Goldman published the canonical derivation in 1999. Twenty-five years later, every variance swap that has ever settled has settled behind a dealer desk.

**Vesta is the first variance-replicating primitive to settle on-chain anywhere.**

A **vLOG** share pays the log-contract value of a price move at expiry:

`2 · [(Sₜ / F − 1) − ln(Sₜ / F)]`per unit of face, replicated VIX-style at mint time from a static portfolio of DeepBook Predict digital ranges and settled trustlessly from a single Pyth-attested settlement price.

The architecture consists of three layers:

- **Execution:** Users sign transactions directly against a Move package—the only path to funds.

- **Replication:** A keeper proposes the replicating portfolio off-chain, but the contract verifies its cost entirely on-chain before issuing a single share.

- **Settlement:** A pure on-chain computation using a Pyth price attestation finalized by Predict's `OracleSVI`.

No off-chain signer ever touches a payout.

Built for the **SuiOverflow 2026 DeepBook Track**,
Vesta exists because Predict provides three primitives that are unavailable together anywhere else on-chain:

- A **Pyth-attested oracle** accessible in a single Move read.

- **Permissionless digital ranges** with mint and redeem.

- A **forward-priced vault AMM**.

The Move package contains seven modules:

- `log_swap`

- `vlog`

- `replication`

- `otc`

- `vault`

- `vyield`

- `signal`

with **55/55 tests passing**, including:

- Replication property tests

- Lifecycle solvency

- OTC mechanics

- Vault NAV invariants

Both `vLOG` and `vYIELD` are standard Sui Coins, enabling third parties to permissionlessly compose:

- Vaults-of-vaults

- Structured product wrappers

- OTC integrations

A full **Next.js** dApp ships the complete product surface—**Trade, Market, Vault, and Portfolio**- while a deterministic `pnpm replay` reproduces every on-chain action end-to-end.

### Trust Roadmap

The custody model is documented and progressively decentralized:

- **Tier 1:** Live hot signer

- **Tier 2:** Nautilus enclave

- **Tier 3:** Seal threshold encryption

- **Tier 4:** `PredictTradeProof` capability pattern (no off-chain custody)

Across every tier, the product interface remains identical:

`mint_shares()
settle()
redeem()
deposit()
harvest()`

About:

**Vortex — PLP yield, minus the crash**

Vortex is a structured-yield vault on Sui's DeepBook Predict. It lets anyone earn the Predict pool's liquidity-provider spread while a signed, on-chain crash hedge caps the left-tail drawdown — turning raw prediction-market LP exposure into a bounded-risk product that ordinary depositors can actually trust.

**The problem**

Supplying liquidity to DeepBook Predict's pool (PLP) earns a steady maker spread — a genuinely attractive carry. But it comes with a hidden cost: the LP is short the tail. A single sharp BTC gap-down can wipe out weeks of accumulated spread in one settlement. The carry is smooth and pleasant right up until it isn't.

That asymmetry — great yield, catastrophic crash risk — is exactly what keeps serious, risk-aware capital out of prediction-market liquidity. There is no easy way to keep the spread while cutting the crash, and no way for a depositor to verify what a strategy is actually doing with their funds. Most "yield" products are black boxes.

**The solution**

Vortex is a one-deposit vault that earns the PLP spread and spends a small sliver of it on out-of-the-money BTC puts as crash insurance — "PLP yield, minus the crash."

You deposit dUSDC once and receive a portable VAULT_SHARE coin. Behind that single deposit, the vault runs two legs:

- Supply leg — supplies idle dUSDC to the Predict pool (predict::supply) to earn the maker spread.

- Hedge leg — spends a small budget minting deep out-of-the-money down-binaries (predict::mint) that pay out precisely when BTC gaps through the strike — the exact scenario that hurts the LP.

The net result is bounded drawdown: you give up a slice of carry in exchange for roughly halving the left tail — and the protection widens sharply under stress. That is a far easier sell to outside liquidity than naked PLP.

Crucially, every allocation is verifiable on-chain. No black box.

**The flow (end-to-end lifecycle)**

Deposit ──► Idle ──► Deployed ──► Settled ──► Redeem ──► Withdraw

 dUSDC      vault     supply +      oracle      sweep      burn

            holds     hedge legs    settles     payout     share

1. Deposit — vault::deposit takes your dUSDC and mints VAULT_SHARE 1:1. Shares are a normal Coin, composable across Sui DeFi (usable as collateral / LP elsewhere).

2. Supply — the strategist signs a supply instruction; the vault verifies it, splits idle dUSDC, calls predict::supply, and banks the returned Coin<PLP>.

3. Hedge — the strategist signs a hedge instruction; the keeper (which owns the PredictManager) funds the manager and calls predict::mint for a deep-OTM down-binary as crash insurance.

4. Roll — while deployed, oracles roll; the keeper rolls fresh legs to keep the position live.

5. Settle & redeem — once an oracle settles, execute_redeem_hedge calls predict::redeem_permissionless and sweeps the payout back into the vault.

6. Withdraw — vault::withdraw burns VAULT_SHARE for the proportional claim on idle dUSDC. Deployed capital is unwound by the keeper first, so withdrawals stay trustless.

**How it works (architecture)**

Vortex is split into three cleanly decoupled parts so that keys never touch the frontend:

- Smart contract — a single, dependency-light Move package, vortex_predict, composing the live deepbook_predict::predict package. Two modules: vault (the vault + signed legs) and vault_share (the share coin). It holds the vault's idle (dUSDC) and plp balances, the share treasury, replay-protection nonce, keeper address, and strategist public key.

- Frontend (Next.js, on Vercel) — read-only-real: every number shown is read straight from the vault object, the public Predict indexer, or live on-chain events. No mock data. It surfaces deposit/withdraw, a live SVI volatility smile + strike ladder, vault composition, an on-chain activity feed, and a redeem panel.

- Keeper (GitHub Actions cron) — runs unattended every 30 minutes. It holds the signing keys (in repo Secrets, never in the frontend), submits signed legs, redeems settled hedges, and rolls fresh ones.

Wallet ──► Frontend (Next.js, read-only-real)

              │ deposit dUSDC

              ▼

        PredictVault<dUSDC> ──mint 1:1──► VAULT_SHARE coin

              ▲  │

   signs every│  │ supply / hedge / withdraw / redeem

   leg (ed25519) │

        Strategist  Keeper (GitHub Actions) ──► DeepBook Predict

**The differentiator — a verifiable strategy**

The strategist can never move funds arbitrarily. Each strategy leg carries an ed25519 signature over an exact, domain-separated byte tuple, which is re-derived and verified on-chain, with a strictly-increasing nonce for replay protection.

supply        : 0x01 | vault_id | nonce | amount

hedge         : 0x02 | vault_id | nonce | oracle_id | expiry | strike | is_up | quantity | budget

withdraw_plp  : 0x03 | vault_id | nonce | plp_amount

Because the message bytes are public and deterministic, anyone can re-derive them and audit exactly which allocation was authorized. Every action also emits a typed event (Deposited, SupplyLegExecuted, HedgeLegExecuted, WithdrawPlpLegExecuted, HedgeRedeemed, Withdrawn), which the /activity page reads directly from chain. This is the core thesis: yield you can audit, not a black box.

**Simulation / proof of the thesis**

A back-test over ~2,000 real settled BTC expiries from the public Predict indexer:

┌──────────────────────┬───────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐

│       Strategy       │  APY  │ Max drawdown (calm / 3× vol stress) │

├──────────────────────┼───────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤

│ Raw PLP              │ ~+20% │ ~0.05% / ~2.6%                      │

├──────────────────────┼───────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤

│ PLP + Hedge (Vortex) │ ~+13% │ ~0.03% / ~1.0%                      │

└──────────────────────┴───────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

The hedge gives up a slice of carry to roughly halve the left tail, and the gap widens sharply under stress — exactly the trade a risk-aware LP wants.

**Live & on-chain (not a mockup)**

- App: [https://vortex-sui.vercel.app](https://vortex-sui.vercel.app)

- Docs: [https://docs-vortex.vercel.app](https://docs-vortex.vercel.app)

- Network: Sui Testnet

- Vault package: 0x185d97299f82a6380e99779eaed8a51833dada528c05b39e3f537eb01a266e83

- PredictVault<dUSDC>: 0xa45ebd4f8c87d7c3d1e4cfe20adb4de9594aa5439bb703685facc7bb7c1314f3

- A funded deposit → supply → hedge → redeem cycle has been executed on the shared vault, with verified transaction digests recorded in [DEMO.md](http://DEMO.md).

**DeepBook Predict track — requirements met**

- ✅ Integrates the Predict contract on testnet — vortex_predict::vault calls predict::supply / mint / withdraw / redeem_permissionless on the live package.

- ✅ Works end-to-end — deposit → signed supply/hedge legs → settle/redeem → withdraw, via the UI + keeper, with verified digests.

- ✅ Simulation result — from real settled BTC history.

Also ships a live SVI surface viewer, a settled-redeem keeper (cron), an on-chain activity feed, and a tokenized share (VAULT_SHARE).

Vortex turns prediction-market LP yield into a product you can actually trust — bounded drawdown, fully automated, and verifiable on-chain.

About:

**Skew - Trade the shape of volatility on Sui.**

Every other prediction market shows you a flat list of yes/no boxes and hides the math. Skew renders the **actual live 3D volatility surface** that DeepBook Predict prices against, and lets you click any point on it to mint that exact bet in one transaction. It's the part every other team is scared to build.

⸻

**The problem:** On-chain prediction markets are opaque and shallow. You see "Will BTC be above $70k? Yes/No" and a price with zero insight into *how* that price was formed or whether it's even internally consistent. Serious traders won't touch a black box, and newcomers bounce off wallets, seed phrases, gas, and faucet hunts before they ever place a trade. DeepBook Predict has a genuinely sophisticated pricing engine (an SVI volatility surface) under the hood but no interface exposes it. Skew is that interface.

⸻

**Features we built:**

- **The live SVI surface (the hero):** A real-time 3D volatility surface strike × expiry × implied vol rendered with React Three Fiber from live on-chain `OracleSVIUpdated` events across 22 expiries. This is the literal math the protocol uses, visualized as a tradeable instrument. Cannot be faked.

- **Time-travel scrub:** Drag a slider and watch the surface *morph* through its real SVI history, then snap back to LIVE on the event stream.

- **No-arbitrage checker:** A live "lie detector" for prices it scans the surface for butterfly and calendar violations (pricing that's mathematically impossible in a healthy market) and flags the exact cells, on real data. A Stress toggle bends the smile to demonstrate it firing on demand.

- **Click-to-mint: **Click any ridge of the surface → a trade ticket pre-fills with that strike/expiry. Price is **chain-authoritative** (read live via `devInspect`, never client-guessed). One button mints a binary or range bet on testnet.

- **Live positions & PnL: **Your DeepBook Predict manager positions, marked against current fair value, with one-click redeem (including permissionless redeem on settled oracles).

- **PLP risk panel: **Vault utilization, exposure, withdrawal-limiter headroom, and a **±N-sigma what-if simulator** that answers the question gating LP capital: "is the vault safe?"

- **Hedge Vault - our own Move contract:** A trustless, custody-free composition router (`predict_hedge`) that opens a "PLP yield minus crash insurance" position in **one transaction call**: fund the manager, mint an out-of-the-money hedge binary, and supply the remainder into the PLP vault.

- **On-chain builder fee - our own Move contract: **A `skew_fee` router that takes a 1% builder fee on-chain at mint, with the live fee rate read from an on-chain `FeeConfig` . A real, sustainable revenue model for Skew powered by DeepBook.

- **Zero-friction onboarding: **Sign in with **Google via Enoki zkLogin** (no seed phrase), transactions are **gasless** (sponsored), and a starter-grant faucet auto-drips **DUSDC** and a little **Sui **to brand-new wallets so they can trade in a few clicks. From nothing to placing a trade in under 30 seconds.

- **Copy-trading & social loop:** A leaderboard → trader profile → one-tap "copy this trade" flow that pre-fills the ticket, share auto-generated result cards and GIFs straight to X, turning prediction markets social.

⸻

**Why it matters for Sui.**

Skew brings professional, quant-desk-grade options infrastructure to Sui, and wraps it in an onboarding so frictionless a non-crypto user can trade in 30 seconds. It pairs DeepBook's deepest primitive (the Predict pricing engine) with Enoki onboarding and an on-chain revenue model into one product that's built to **redeploy on mainnet with a single config switch** (every contract ID is network-keyed). It's not a hollow UI on top of the stack, it's a new front door to it.

⸻

**Sui Stack & Move Contracts**

- DeepBook Predict

- Two of our Move contracts, deployed on Sui Testnet

About:

Trade prediction markets with leverage on DeepBook Predict.

Most prediction markets let you bet *yes* or *no* — and that's it. No leverage. No pro tools. No way to earn from the other side unless you're the house.

**LeverX changes that.**

We built the missing layer on top of DeepBook Predict: a place where you can bet on where BTC (and other assets soon) will land, use up to 10× leverage, set limit prices, track your PnL live — and if you'd rather not trade, deposit into a pool and earn when others borrow.

Think of it as prediction markets with a margin account — live on Sui testnet today.

**What can you do?**

🎯 Trade with conviction

- Pick UP (price finishes above a strike), DOWN (below), or RANGE (inside a band)

- Put down as little as 0.1 dUSDC, scale up to 10× leverage

- See a real chart, live prices, and whether you're winning or losing *before* expiry

- Place market orders for speed or limit orders when you want a better price

💰 Earn without trading

Don't want to pick direction? Add dUSDC to the pool. Traders borrow from it when they use leverage — and LPs earn from that activity. Deposit and withdraw anytime from the Vault page.

🤖 Let Jarvis trade for you

Turn on **Jarvis AI Agent** in the app. Set your rules — max leverage, how much of your balance to use, risk level — and Jarvis scans your account every few minutes, manages open positions, and looks for new opportunities. You stay in control; Jarvis does the busywork.

📱 Trade from Telegram

Link your wallet once, then trade from chat:

`/up 70k 5x 10`

`/markets`

`/balance`

No browser tab required. Great for quick calls on the go.

**Why we built it**

DeepBook Predict already prices every strike on a live volatility surface — that's powerful infrastructure. But traders still need:

- **Leverage** — size a view without putting up the full premium

- **A real UI** — charts, portfolio, order flow, not just raw contract calls

- **Liquidity for borrowers** — a vault that backs leveraged positions

- **Automation** — keepers that fill limits, close risky positions, and keep the system healthy

LeverX delivers all four as one product you can use end-to-end today.

**What's next**

- **Mainnet** — redeploy on day one when Predict launches and switch completely to a premium RPC

- **More composability** — vault shares as collateral across Sui DeFi (margin, lending, structured products)

- **Smarter bots** — vol-arb between Predict and other venues, dynamic liquidation thresholds.

- **Better mobile & social** — streaks, leaderboards, group trading in Telegram

About:

CapletFi is a hedged carry vault for DeepBook Predict on Sui: deposit DUSDC, receive capDUSDC vault shares, earn PLP carry, and stay hedged when BTC breaks. Under the hood the vault runs a repeatable structured-product loop: it prices a downside binary hedge from DeepBook Predict, supplies the idle buffer into DeepBook Predict's liquidity-provider position (the PLP carry leg), settles against the oracle, withdraws realized value, and publishes receipt-backed accounting.

The project turns DeepBook Predict from a low-level trading primitive into a treasury-readable structured product. DeepBook Predict is load-bearing: remove Predict and CapletFi loses the hedge, the PLP carry leg, the settlement asset, and the call path. The live vault uses predict::get_trade_amounts, predict::mint, predict::supply, predict::redeem_permissionless, predict::withdraw, and predict_manager::withdraw.

The current submission runs on Sui testnet because DeepBook Predict is testnet-only during the submission window. DUSDC is the DeepBook Predict test asset. CapletFi is risk-defined, not principal-guaranteed, not risk-free, and not fixed APY. The V5 vault adds an onchain ask gate: before opening a hedge, the vault reads DeepBook Predict's executable ask and rejects fills above the policy ceiling. The prose maps to the chain one to one. The pinned proof transaction ANpQxwFUgnmNuFe8awduRTXZFHrDUjwsLipKKum38ALu emits, in one roll, the ask-gate check as caplet_vault::HedgeFillChecked, the hedge mint as predict::PositionMinted, the PLP supply as predict::Supplied, and the cycle open as caplet_vault::CycleOpened. The close receipt CxSjH67kWSReRNFXLga5TmzDfiPbSi4ggHbnBdScf17C emits the withdrawal as predict::Withdrawn and the cycle close as caplet_vault::CycleClosed.

The proof surface includes a live vault, capDUSDC shares, self-serve deposit and withdrawal, a non-holder settlement path, holder-directed manager-credit withdrawal, Walrus term sheets and settlement reports, proof JSON, AI-readable llms.txt, and verifier scripts that replay the Sui and Walrus receipts.

About:

**Hunchbook. Bet your hunch, or be the house.**

Hunchbook is a crypto price prediction app on Sui, built on DeepBook Predict. You can bet on where BTC is going in the next hour, or you can put money into a vault and earn the same way the house earns.

**The problem**

Most prediction apps only let you play one role. You place a bet, the platform keeps an edge, and you never get to sit on the other side. The people who actually supply the money behind the bets are usually big and hidden. Hunchbook opens both seats. Anyone can take a hunch, and anyone can be the house that backs those hunches.

**How a bet works**

Pick a market. Think BTC finishes above a price? Choose Above. Think it stays inside a band? Choose Range. You can set any strike in one dollar steps and pick a short expiry, often under an hour. Every market is priced live using an SVI volatility surface, so you see your real win chance and payout before you commit. Settlement is simple. Your payout is locked in the moment you buy, the market settles only at expiry, and you either win the set amount or lose your stake. There is a flat 1% fee.

Signing in is the easy part. You log in with Google through zkLogin and Enoki, so there is no wallet to install and no seed phrase to remember. Gas is sponsored too, so the app is free to use and most people never notice they are on chain.

**Be the house**

Put money into the vault and you become the other side of every bet. When you deposit you get pfShare, a token that tracks your slice of the vault, its value, and its yield. The vault looks after itself. It buys cheap insurance on the edges so one wild move cannot wipe it out, and a keeper service runs it around the clock. The keeper prices the pool, puts idle money to work, and manages the insurance. You deposit and watch it run.

**The flywheel**

The two sides feed each other. When bettors lose, that money flows to the vault. When the vault grows, it can back more bets. More bets mean more fees and more yield. We are building a small meter that shows this loop live, so you can watch betting activity turn into vault returns.

**The fun layer**

On top of the basics there is an Auto Studio. You set a budget, a goal, and a stop loss, then let a strategy run for you. There are presets like Steady, Streak, and Press, and every round shows clear odds and honest numbers. A stop loss is required before any run can start. There is also a one tap roll that places the same bet again on the next round. We keep this part honest on purpose. The brand should feel as fun as Stake but as trustworthy as Aave, so responsible play limits are built in, not bolted on.

**What works today**

Everything above is live and running on testnet right now. The full trading loop, accounts and stats, leaderboards, daily streaks, the complete hedged vault with deposits, withdrawals, insurance, and the keeper, and the Auto Studio with real strategies. This is not a slideshow. It is a working table with players, a house, and the machine that keeps both going.

**How it earns**

A 1% fee on every bet, live today. Vault fees of 20% on performance and 1% on management, live today. Auto play adds more bets per session as it rolls out.

**What is next**

A mainnet launch is the first job after the hackathon. After that, more assets and time frames, a tokenized vault share that other Sui apps can build on, referrals, and weekly tournaments.

**Links**
Live app at [hunchbook.vercel.app](http://hunchbook.vercel.app). Code at github.com/keshav2800/hunchbook. Demo video [https://youtu.be/1P59__bkneA](https://youtu.be/1P59__bkneA)

About:

DEEPVOL — Trade Movement, Not Just Direction

Sui Overflow 2026 Submission

A Predict-native volatility trading terminal on Sui (Testnet), built on top of DeepBook Predict. Instead of asking only "will the price go up or down?", DeepVol lets you express a view on how much an asset will move.

DeepVol is non-custodial: every transaction is signed by the user's own wallet, and underlying positions stay in the user's own PredictManager. The frontend never touches private keys.

--------------------------------------------------

THE PROBLEM WE'RE SOLVING

Directional prediction is hard. A lot of the time you don't have strong up-or-down conviction — you just believe something big is about to happen, or that things will stay calm. That's a volatility view, not a direction view, and most prediction markets give you no clean way to express it.

DeepVol packages DeepBook Predict's binary primitives into a structured BTC MOVE product, and also exposes the raw UP / DOWN / RANGE primitives directly, so you can say "BTC will move a lot" or "BTC will stay in this range" instead of "BTC will finish above X."

--------------------------------------------------

CORE PRODUCTS

1. BTC MOVE (Flagship Composed Product)

A MOVE receipt is two legs: a long UP above an upper strike, plus a long DOWN below a lower strike. You win when BTC moves far enough outside the range by expiry, in either direction (direction-agnostic). Both legs are minted in a single PTB via receipt::buy_move_receipt (DUSDC), which creates a non-custodial MoveReceipt object recording the full structure. A Create Fee of 0.30% of premium is charged into the DeepVol ProtocolVault.

2. UP / DOWN (Raw Binary Primitives)

Direct DeepBook Predict binary positions. UP wins if BTC finishes above the strike; DOWN wins below. No receipt, no Create Fee — single directional building blocks.

3. RANGE (Raw Range Primitive)

Via predict::mint_range (DUSDC). The mirror of MOVE: wins when BTC expires inside the selected interval. No receipt, no VolSeries, no Create Fee.

--------------------------------------------------

WHAT'S DEPLOYED ON SUI TESTNET

• DeepVol Move package: 0xe9b45e8d06406bb935bf5e3260ac9b82491ca158775861660be34881e17a4fa0

• Publish digest: 3HZ88SZZsUpmgJqxCAqzKxi6ceeD1pkrKXnprDS36DFi

• ProtocolVault (DUSDC): 0x1b9174645d70ac4beb877a06541d184b8c3bec46b81ccca2de38495979c25a658f98923407bf697

• Modules: errors, fees, receipt, series, vault

• Quote asset: DUSDC (6 decimals)

• Network: Sui Testnet only

• Official Dependency: Built on official DeepBook Predict Testnet package 0xf5ea2b3749c65d6e56507cc35388719aadb28f9cab873696a2f8687f5c785138.

--------------------------------------------------

END-TO-END ON-CHAIN VALIDATION

Every flow below was executed against Sui Testnet with a real wallet signature.

• BTC MOVE buy (fresh end-to-end): 6sq8ZydZS3sLXNU6Y31gxSqBniVdf7SEXMwiKzJmjbXg

• BTC MOVE buy (browser wallet): A6YB62BqMmWsQeEZUoh4qYAA6n4RMqnih5TtHRdadfGn

• UP primitive mint: 4JCQ9ZCPRfWugiRhQsU6Y3rAaeT3CyYMMGZ4XnoCWcy9

• DOWN primitive mint: 4XU2145PwZNm1Qn3NtVkEdKZt9VPjiZeY5vNwTcy7jnH

• Guided redeem: HeHNeZ95oymZzmA2ZpdjkvJgCaA9s5DzL7qs6aCgbJbJ

• Validated MoveReceipt object: 0x85d803ae6b8a66f6d0e0772e8906d8076dea210de3eaa322d712db58eb6ff869

--------------------------------------------------

ARCHITECTURE

Structured as an npm-workspaces monorepo. A shared headless trading state machine drives every product.

Workspace Layout:

• apps/deepvol-open-design: Open Design app (Vite 7 + React 19 + TW 4)

• packages/deepvol-trading-react: Shared trading machines (MOVE / UP / DOWN / RANGE, gated flow)

• @deepvol/sdk, types, config: SDK, type definitions, and configuration

• DeepBook Predict on Sui (Testnet): Pricing, oracle, vault, and settlement layer

The Gated Execution Flow (no wallet prompt until every check passes):

1. Active market discovery (find the live BTC market).

2. Mintability validation (confirm the position can actually be minted).

3. Fresh quote (pull pricing within a freshness window).

4. On-chain preflight (run a devInspect dry-run within a freshness window).

5. Wallet review / sign (only now does the user's wallet open).

Tech Stack: Sui Move, DeepBook Predict, Vite 7, React 19, Tailwind CSS 4, @mysten/dapp-kit, @mysten/sui, @tanstack/react-query, TypeScript strict mode.

--------------------------------------------------

WHAT MAKES THIS SUI-NATIVE

• Built directly on a brand-new primitive: DeepBook Predict's UP / DOWN / RANGE binary markets.

• PTB composition: buy_move_receipt mints both UP and DOWN legs and charges the Create Fee in a single atomic transaction.

• Object-centric receipt: The MoveReceipt is a Sui object owned by the user, not a custodied database row.

• Non-custodial by construction: Underlying binary positions remain inside the user's PredictManager; DeepVol holds no user funds.

• Shared ProtocolVault: Create Fees accrue on-chain via the shared ProtocolVault (DUSDC) object, with on-chain accounting.

--------------------------------------------------

HOW TO VERIFY IN UNDER A MINUTE

Step 1: Clone the repository

git clone [https://github.com/StarryDeserts/DeepVol.git](https://github.com/StarryDeserts/DeepVol.git)

cd DeepVol

Step 2: Install and Run

npm install

npm run dev:open-design

Step 3: Test the flow

Open the printed dev URL, connect a Sui Testnet wallet with some Testnet SUI for gas and a PredictManager funded with DUSDC, and walk through the gated MOVE / UP / DOWN / RANGE flow. No .env setup required — all Testnet config is baked into @deepvol/config.

Type-check / build / UI tests verification:

• npm run typecheck:open-design (passes)

• npm run build:open-design (passes)

• npm --workspace apps/deepvol-open-design run test:open-design-ui (93 UI tests pass)

**Real-world application.** CallIt is built to be **the game people open every day**: win streaks that level up a flame, badges, a weekly leaderboard, and Telegram result cards that land in your group chat and pull the next player in — a social loop that turns one bet into a habit. Under it sits the part everyone else gets wrong: prediction markets fail normal users at the front door (wallets, gas, order books), so we compressed the whole thing into call it → watch it → get paid. Free practice stack on testnet; builder codes monetize each settled call at mainnet.

Link Telegram and your account survives a cleared browser. Ten seconds from landing to a funded on-chain account (sponsored session wallet). Live odds shimmer as the vol surface updates. Hold-to-confirm cash-outs at the live bid. Confetti settlements and Telegram result cards — and if you miss one while away, the celebration replays the moment you return. If an oracle feed goes stale, betting disables itself with an honest `ORACLE STALE` state instead of a failed transaction.

**Technology.** Typed Move bindings generated from the deployed package; `deposit + mint` in one PTB; keeper auto-claims via `redeem_permissionless`; every quote is a devInspect call to `predict::get_trade_amounts`. Expiries, strikes, and underlyings are discovered dynamically — the app survived testnet's mid-hackathon switch from weekly to 15-minute expiries with zero code changes.

Web login is a single-use nonce confirmed through the bot — Telegram's own identity, no third-party widget. Verified end-to-end: 64 unit + 14 Playwright tests against live testnet in CI, and every demo transaction on-chain and checkable.

About:

### **DeepPilot**

DeepPilot is an AI RiskOps cockpit for DeepBook Predict. It helps a user go from a plain-language idea such as "Bet 10 DUSDC that BTC will be down by tonight" to a live market review, risk checks, wallet-ready transaction preview, and final wallet signing path.

It is built for people who are new to prediction markets as well as operators who need a safer review flow before signing. The product does not ask users to understand every Predict object, oracle window, quote expiry, Trading Balance rule, or gas condition upfront. It turns those protocol details into a guided review.

### **Why DeepPilot Exists**

Prediction markets are powerful, but the first trade is hard. A new user has to answer several questions before they can act:

- Which BTC prediction market is active now?

- Which expiry should I use?

- Is the oracle fresh, or is the market already stale?

- How much DUSDC will the position cost?

- Is there enough Trading Balance in the PredictManager?

- Will the wallet be on the right Sui network with enough gas?

- Is this a single trade, a redeem action, or a multi-leg strategy?

DeepPilot was created to remove that operational burden. The user can start with natural language in Web or Telegram, while DeepPilot converts the request into a constrained Predict review, fetches live market data, runs deterministic risk checks, and only then opens a wallet signing path when the review is still valid.

### **What It Solves**

DeepPilot solves three practical problems:

- Onboarding friction: users can ask in natural language instead of manually assembling oracle ids, expiry choices, market keys, and transaction details.

- Pre-sign risk: every trade or strategy is checked against live Predict server status, oracle freshness, vault utilization, quote availability, Trading Balance, wallet network, and gas readiness before signing.

- Cross-channel handoff: a user can begin in Telegram, receive a Review & Sign link, and continue in the Web app where the quote and risk checks are refreshed before wallet confirmation.

### **What Users Can Do**

- Discover live BTC Predict markets in `/markets`, with expiry filters, quick risk labels, vault context, and chart history.

- Ask market questions in `/trade`; DeepPilot can answer with retrieved Predict/news/project context instead of forcing every message into a transaction.

- Place single-trade intents in plain language, for example: `Buy 10 DUSDC BTC UP on the next active market`.

- Draft multi-leg strategies in plain language, for example: `Split 20 DUSDC across the next 1h, 2h, and 3h BTC UP markets`.

- Review Guardian results before signing: `allow`, `reduce`, or `block`, with the reason shown to the user.

- Create or link a PredictManager, check Trading Balance, and sign wallet transactions for supported execution flows.

- Use Telegram commands such as `/login`, `/markets`, `/news BTC`, `/trade ...`, and`/vault LP... /strategy ...` to start from chat and finish in Web review.

- Track local receipts, manager state, Trading Balance, positions, PnL, settlement status, and redeem/funding actions in `/profile`.

### **User Benefit**

For a first-time prediction-market user, DeepPilot changes the experience from "learn the protocol first" to "describe what you want, then review the generated plan." The user still makes the trading decision and still controls the wallet signature, but the confusing parts of the protocol are surfaced as readable checks.

For a more experienced user, DeepPilot reduces repeated operational work: market discovery, quote refresh, strategy leg construction, wallet preflight, and receipt tracking are all placed in one workflow.

**VWATCH**

A real-time volatility terminal for DeepBook Predict on Sui.

VWATCH reads the DeepBook Predict event stream from Sui testnet, decodes raw SVI calibration parameters, and renders the live implied volatility surface - strike × expiry × IV, alongside vault risk metrics, arbitrage checks, scenario simulations, and on-chain exposure heatmaps. Every number is computed from on-chain events. No mock data. No interpolation. The protocol speaks; the terminal listens.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026 (DeepBook Predict track).

**What it does:**

- **3D vol surface** streamed from `oracle::OracleSVIUpdated` events with time-travel replay

- **Vol analytics**: term structure, skew curve, Gatheral arbitrage checks, IV vs realized vol comparison

- **PLP risk dashboard**: vault health, utilization zones, withdrawal limiter, max payouts, oracle freshness

- **Scenario simulator**: BTC ±2/5/10% shocks with asymmetric crash-skew model

- **Risk exposure heatmap**: per-strike per-expiry net call/put exposure from on-chain `oracle_matrices`

- **Smile shape alerts**: live notifications when SVI shape changes (SMILE → SKEW → SMIRK)

- **Embeddable widgets**: drop-in iframes for partner Sui frontends — no SDK, no API key

-

**Stack:** Next.js 16 + Three.js (frontend, Vercel) · Node.js + `@mysten/sui` (backend, Railway) · WebSocket pipeline · Sui testnet RPC.

About:

KOVA is a permissionless intent and solver settlement layer built natively on Sui, using DeepBook v3 as its liquidity backbone.

**What it does**

Instead of swapping directly, users submit intents — signed commitments that say "I want to trade X for at least Y, before deadline Z." These intents are escrowed on-chain inside a KovaIntent object. An off-chain solver network watches for new intents, routes them through DeepBook, and fills them atomically. If the trade clears the user's minimum output floor, the intent settles. If not, it reverts — the user's funds never leave escrow.

**How the settlement works**

The core mechanic is a hot potato pattern in Move. When a solver attempts a fill, the contract issues a FillReceipt — a resource with no drop ability. The solver must resolve it within the same transaction by delivering the output tokens. This makes partial fills and fund misappropriation structurally impossible, not just policy. The surplus above the user's floor is split 50/50 between the user and the solver (with 5bps to the protocol), giving solvers a direct economic incentive to route well.

**DeepBook integration**

DeepBook is the sole execution venue. The SDK exposes a shared getQuote() function (wrapping getQuoteQuantityOut / getBaseQuantityOut) that both the frontend and the solver import from the same package — so the price the user sees and the price the solver executes at come from identical pool/direction resolution. There's no routing drift between UI and execution.

**Solver infrastructure**

The solver bot is a TypeScript process that subscribes to Sui events, filters open intents, fetches a live DeepBook quote, checks profitability against a configurable minProfitBps threshold, and builds a programmable transaction block to fill. It logs each fill to Walrus for a tamper-evident audit trail. The solver is self-hosting ready — it binds an HTTP health endpoint so it can run free on Render (or any host that requires a listening port), and exposes live fill stats that the frontend reads directly.

**Permissionless solver set**

Any wallet can become a solver by calling registry::register and staking SUI. There's no allowlist or admin approval. The frontend includes a self-service staking UI — connect your wallet, enter an amount, sign. The on-chain SolverRecord tracks your fill count and stake. Unstaking is also one click and returns funds immediately.

**Frontend**

Built with Next.js App Router, @mysten/dapp-kit for wallet integration, and TanStack Query for live chain state. The swap form fetches a real-time DeepBook quote with 400ms debounce and auto-fills the minimum output field with slippage applied. Users can override the floor manually without losing their custom value when they change slippage presets.

**Stack**

- Smart contracts: Move on Sui (18 tests, all passing)

- SDK: TypeScript, @mysten/deepbook-v3, shared between frontend and solver

- Solver: Node.js, Walrus audit logging, HTTP health server

- Frontend: Next.js 15, TanStack Query, Mysten dapp-kit

- Infra: Vercel (frontend), Render (solver)

**Testnet package: **0xf34836a0a26c0a40b1862c4d2a43354e9983f805cb14f67158f0dc7a954edbb1

**Registry: **0xbc95b8f3edab021b4c60be2c21df778062e3bcee440a4fcb4582b1da3824c544

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/b470f0b8-a622-4f01-83ae-f0b7a319ab60/6c7a17e9-a0f0-444b-a54e-8c38b66a3741.png

About:

**APEX** is a structured-product layer on *DeepBook Predict* - it turns raw PLP liquidity into a vault that earns the house's yield with built-in crash insurance.

**The problem.**
*DeepBook Predict's* liquidity pool (PLP) earns fees from every trade, but it sits on the other side of every position. In a sharp BTC move it absorbs the entire left tail - and that uncapped tail risk is exactly what keeps serious liquidity providers away. Raw PLP is a hard sell.

**APEX's answer.**
APEX is a structured-product layer on top of Predict. Its flagship is the PLP + Hedge vault, which in a single atomic transaction:

- Supplies the bulk of a deposit to the Predict vault (predict::supply), earning PLP yield, and

- Mints an out-of-the-money downside binary (predict::mint), funded from a slice of that yield, that pays out exactly when the market crashes.

The result is PLP yield with a built-in floor - a modest give-up in headline APY for a roughly halved drawdown through a crash. That's a product outside LPs can underwrite, not a raw directional bet.

**Proof, not promises.**
APEX makes the strategy legible and auditable:

- A simulation runs PLP vs PLP+Hedge through two BTC crashes, quantifying the drawdown reduction.

- A live SVI volatility surface, reconstructed straight from on-chain OracleSVI parameters, shows the vol the hedge is priced against.

- A PLP risk dashboard surfaces live vault utilization and a ±5σ stress test - directly answering the "is PLP safe?" question that gates real LP capital.

Everything runs live on Sui testnet against the real *DeepBook Predict* contracts: create/find a PredictManager, deposit DUSDC, mint positions against live sub-hour BTC oracles, settle, claim winnings (predict::redeem), and withdraw to wallet - plus the atomic supply-and-hedge vault deployment. Sitting underneath is a live prediction-market floor with a real-time settlement countdown, so the vault is built on a market people can actually trade.

About:

Predict Strategy Copilot is a strategy layer on top of DeepBook

Predict that makes a professional-grade, volatility-priced

prediction market usable by anyone.

DeepBook Predict prices short-term BTC bets the way professional

options desks do — using a live volatility surface (SVI). Powerful,

but unusable for non-experts: strikes, expiries, implied volatility

are a wall. The liquidity is there; the users can't reach it.

We fix that. The app reads live SVI parameters from the Predict

oracle, computes the standard deviation of price, and turns it into

three plain-language strategies anyone understands:

- Stay in range — bet the price holds steady (±1σ)

- Price up — directional bet near the current price

- Crash hedge — cheap insurance against a sharp drop (−2σ)

Each card shows exactly what you pay, your max win, and time to

settlement — no jargon. Users mint binary and range positions,

track P&L, and claim winnings, all on Sui testnet against the real

DeepBook Predict contracts.

Beyond single bets, we added a full social-trading loop:

- Leaderboard — ranks real investors by actual on-chain P&L (no

fake data)

- Copy-trade — follow a top investor and copy their bets in one

tap; you always sign yourself (non-custodial)

- Auto-Vault — a keeper bot auto-redeems each settled round and

re-enters the strategy (custodial demo; trustless delegation is

future work, pending protocol support for owner-delegated minting)

Everything runs end-to-end on Sui testnet. Built to redeploy on

mainnet day one.

*Note: we deploy no custom Move package — this is a frontend + tooling + social layer built entirely on the existing DeepBook Predict package *`0xf5ea2b…785138`* on Sui Testnet.*

About:

### Voltz: Leveraged Trading for DeepBook Predict

**Description:** Voltz turns DeepBook Predict's 1× binary markets into a leveraged trading terminal with two ways to participate. Traders go long or short on BTC strike and range outcomes at up to 4× leverage using market or limit orders, backed by a purpose-built on-chain risk engine. Liquidity providers deposit into a Move lending vault to earn real yield from borrow interest and trading fees, funding the leverage traders use. Live end-to-end on Sui testnet.

**What we built:**

- Leveraged long/short up to 4× on BTC strikes and range markets, via market and limit orders

- dUSDC lending vault (Move): LPs supply capital and earn yield; traders borrow against collateral

- Atomic execution (PTBs): deposit, borrow, position mint, and lien registration in one transaction

- Multi-position support via per-(wallet, market) liens

- Gasless UX through Shinami Invisible Wallets (Google/email login, sponsored txns)

- Autonomous keepers for health monitoring, liquidation, TP/SL execution, and reserve rebalancing

- Full quote → open → manage → close/liquidate lifecycle live on Sui testnet

**Risk engine (built for binary-market risk):**

- Hot-potato borrow receipts force in-transaction loan repayment

- Health-based liquidation at 45% with a 2% bounty, closing positions before equity collapses so the vault recovers its capital and the loan is repaid

- Minimum initial health checks block underwater positions

- Time-decay leverage caps near expiry (4× → 3× → 2× → forced unwind)

- Pre-expiry auto-close removes exposure to the 0/100 settlement jump

- 5% reserve buffer protects LPs via a loss waterfall (reserve → LP NAV)

**How we make money:**

- Share of trader borrow interest (~10% default)

- Treasury share of the 10% performance fee on profitable trades (configurable via `voltz_config`)

- All flow into an on-chain treasury (admin cap); fees cover gas sponsorship, so revenue scales with volume and open interest

**How LPs make money:**
LPs earn yield from real trading activity: borrow interest paid by leveraged traders (kinked-rate model), the 0.5% opening fee, and a share of the 10% performance fee on winning trades. These accrue into the vault and grow NAV, so VVT shares appreciate over time, with a 5% reserve absorbing bad debt first to protect principal.

**Roadmap:**

- Mainnet launch aligned with DeepBook Predict going live

- Advanced engine with real-time market making and execution routing

- Analytics dashboards for flow, leverage exposure, and liquidations

- Marketing push: community campaigns, trader incentives, and LP onboarding to drive volume and TVL

Architecture: In one line: LPs fund the Lending Vault → the Router borrows from it to open leveraged positions on DeepBook Predict → the Risk Engine + Keepers protect that capital (liens, liquidation,

auto-close) → interest and fees route through Config into the Treasury and back into vault NAV, so VVT shares appreciate and people earn .

About:

Automark is the asset management layer for Predict, DeepBook's new prediction market on Sui.

Experts bring the edge, allocators get the exposure, and Automark connects the two.

---
**
The gap**

Automark connects financial expertise with idle capital on Predict, DeepBook's new prediction market.

Experts and small funds have alpha. They read markets and they know how to operate sharp instruments. What they don't have is balance sheet.

Investors have the opposite problem: capital sitting idle, with no way to operate markets they don't understand.

Predict makes this mismatch acute. It opens a new market on Sui (BTC outcomes, priced on-chain) that quant desks know how to use and the rest of the chain doesn't.

Automark lets one side operate it on behalf of the other.

---

**Why this matters now**

Prediction and options markets on-chain have historically stalled at small TVL. The instrument itself was rarely the whole story.; it was that these markets could not reach capital outside a handful of specialist desks. Polymarket, Lyra, and dYdX options were all powerful and all stuck below the ceiling that distribution sets.

Predict is the first prediction market embedded in a major orderbook venue on Sui. The instrument is sharper, the venue is composable, and the rails are already in place.

What's missing is the asset management layer that lets non-specialist capital actually use it.

---

**What we built**

Automark is a full protocol stack on Sui. It includes a realtime indexing pipeline, a depositor app, a creator studio, an SDK, and a runner for programmatic access, all built around one on-chain core.

That core is the vault primitive: one Move contract of roughly 3,000 lines, of which every vault is an instance. Using a single construction is a deliberate choice rather than a limitation. It is precisely what lets every vault carry the same structural protections by default. The primitive is hardened once, and every vault inherits those protections in every configuration. The complexity lives in one place instead of fragmenting across a dozen bespoke contracts.

The primitive is organized as plug-and-socket:

- The container holds capital, mints the vault's branded share Coin, tracks NAV, and owns every policy field: anti-rug caps, scoped permissions, authorized witness type, operator address, fee and risk parameters.

- The strategy is a separate module that the creator deploys. It is pure logic, with no custody and no stored authority. It invokes operations on the container, and the container validates every call inline.

Operators decide what to do and when. The contract decides whether it is allowed. That separation is what makes the model work. Strategy bytecode can evolve continuously (thresholds, rebalancing logic, new market regimes) while capital, depositors, share Coin identity, and history stay the same. The vault grows with its operator instead of fragmenting TVL across new deployments.

Every vault inherits the same protections by construction: lagged on-chain NAV that makes oracle manipulation economically expensive, unconditional emergency redemption against the liquid balance, uniform anti-rug caps, and timelocked governance with a depositor exit window before any policy change takes effect.

---
**
Why the protections are enforceable, not reputational**

What makes the protection real is the Move type system itself:

- The witness type authorized to operate the vault is a field of the container. Move's module encapsulation guarantees that only the plugged strategy module can construct it. Bypassing it is structurally impossible, not merely a matter of convention.

- Authorization runs inline at every operation. There is no transferable, persistable, or replayable auth token that could be stolen.

- The strategy module never receives capital. The container exposes operations that keep custody throughout.

- Anti-rug caps are asserted at vault creation against immutable bounds. Strategy upgrades cannot widen them.

A strategy swap only touches logic; it cannot touch policy. There is no atomic surprise, because governance changes are visible before they take effect.

---
**
Each vault issues its own branded asset**

Every vault publishes its own fungible Coin using the SpringSui pattern: one OTW per vault, with its own symbol, icon, and decimals. The depositor doesn't hold a generic "share." They hold a branded asset that represents that specific vault's performance.

Because it is a standard Sui Coin, a vault position is composable with the rest of the ecosystem out of the box:

- traded on Cetus, Aftermath, Kriya, or any Sui DEX

- posted as collateral on Suilend, Navi, or Scallop

- paired into LPs for liquidity provision

- borrowed against on Bucket to mint stable

- custodied in any standard multisig

There is no wrapper, no adapter, and no special integration path. Composability comes from the type itself.

Vaults are not silos. Because the share is a real Coin, a single position can do more than one job at once. Consider a depositor in a BTC-skew vault who holds its share, vSKEW. That depositor can post vSKEW as collateral on a lending market, borrow stables against it, and put those stables to work elsewhere (a second vault, an LP, or a yield source), all while vSKEW continues to track the original vault's performance. The collateral keeps compounding while the borrowed capital works on top of it. None of this requires permission from Automark or a custom integration; it follows directly from the share being a standard Sui Coin.

---
**
Built for the agentic economy**

Two surfaces make Automark programmable.

The Automark Runner ([github.com/automark-protocol/runner](https://github.com/automark-protocol/runner)) s the operator side. Running `***npx create-automark-runner my-vault`*** scaffolds an off-chain runtime that executes a strategy. You write a plugin, a function that decides when to mint, redeem, or rebalance, and the runner schedules it, signs as the vault's operator, and submits the transactions. The engine ships vendored, with no hidden framework, and its only dependencies are the SDK, @mysten/sui, and dotenv.

The Automark SDK ([github.com/automark-protocol/automark-sdk](https://github.com/automark-protocol/automark-sdk)), TypeScript-first and in active development, lets servers, trading bots, treasury systems, and AI agents interact with vaults natively: discovering them, depositing and redeeming, streaming NAV and position updates in realtime, and reacting to on-chain events.

The consequence is that capital decisions no longer have to be human. An agent can route stablecoins into a Verified-tier vault when on-chain conditions trigger. A treasury bot can rebalance across multiple strategies automatically. An AI agent can monitor performance signals and reallocate without intervention.

The vault primitive's structural protections apply equally to any caller, human or machine. The agent operates under the same anti-rug floor as any depositor. It can be wrong about a strategy, but it cannot be drained by one.

As more capital becomes programmatic, the asset management layer has to be too. Predict gives Sui a powerful new market, and the SDK and runner are the surfaces that let the rest of the economy, human or machine, flow capital into it on the same rails.

---
**
What's running today (testnet)**

Vault primitive deployed on testnet, with binary and range trade flows on Predict end-to-end: mint, redeem, lagged NAV snapshot, emergency redemption, governance with timelock and exit window.

- Realtime indexer pipeline streaming vault events to the consumer app.

- Consumer app for depositors: discover vaults, deposit and withdraw, and follow live performance.

- Studio for creators: a strategy template builder with a configure, compile, deploy flow that produces a custom strategy module plugged into a freshly created vault, all from the browser.

- Public API surface, snapshot-tested.

---
**
Why Sui + DeepBook**

The vault primitive is only enforceable because Move makes it so. Encapsulation, the witness pattern, hot-potato, and inline authorization checks turn the protection model from a promise into a guarantee. Other VMs can express the shape, but only Move can guarantee it at the type level.

Predict is also the first venue that makes a market like this composable rather than siloed. Vaults emit standard Sui Coins, they plug into the rest of the ecosystem natively, and they operate against a venue maintained by DeepBook itself. The composition is not a bridge; it is the default.

Automark turns Predict from a specialist primitive into infrastructure that non-specialist capital can actually use.

About:

**deepskew is a quant terminal for DeepBook Predict on Sui.**

DeepBook Predict puts options-style volatility trading on-chain. An oracle publishes a volatility surface, a liquidity vault underwrites every position, and traders mint and redeem binary options against it block by block. It all settles on Sui in the open. The problem is that none of it is readable. A raw SVI parameter set tells you nothing about whether the surface admits arbitrage. A vault balance tells you nothing about whether it survives a sharp move in BTC. The information is on-chain. The meaning is not.

**deepskew is the legibility layer.** It reads the live surface, the vault, and the settlement flow straight from the chain and turns each one into a single clear read with the numbers that back it underneath. It is the screen a desk would keep open while the market is live. And it closes the loop: where a read leads to an action, you act on-chain without leaving the verdict.

**Why it exists**

A traditional options desk has a wall of tools for this: a surface viewer, an arbitrage monitor, a risk engine, a blotter. On-chain volatility has none of it. The data is public and the protocol is permissionless, which is the whole promise, but in practice you still have to decode fixed-point parameters, run the vol math yourself, and rebuild state from a stream of events before you can answer a basic question like "is this surface fair" or "can the vault take this trade." That gap is what keeps serious flow out. deepskew closes it by doing the decoding, the math, and the reconstruction for you, and by refusing to show a number without showing what it means.

**Two reading modes**

The desk ships in Pro by default, the dense instrument above. A Noob/Pro toggle (in the command strip or the Cmd-K palette, persisted across sessions) switches the whole desk to a plain-language lens without touching the data underneath. In Noob mode every panel folds to one card: a verdict sentence, the panel's own chart, and a few plain figures, with the full Pro body one click away behind "Details." The two action panels become simple forms wired to the same on-chain handlers, so a first-time reader can still mint a position or supply the vault. Same live state, two levels of density. Legibility for the quant who wants every parameter, and for the LP who just wants to know whether the vault is safe to back.

**What's inside**

One persistent desk with seven routed views. Market selection, wallet, and the live query cache live in the shell, so moving between views keeps the data warm.

- **Desk.** The single-viewport instrument: the full BTC vol surface in 3D, the current smile with its butterfly and calendar arbitrage verdict, the oracle state, the PLP vault with sigma stress, and a live tape of mints and redeems.

- **Vol Analytics.** The smile across log-moneyness, skew and term structure with 25-delta risk reversals and butterflies, the risk-neutral density the surface prices via Breeden-Litzenberger, and smile dynamics over time.

- **Flow & Edge.** Every fill scored against its model-fair price (the digital `N(d2)` from the live surface) to show the vol-risk-premium captured fill by fill, plus settlement calibration, whale flow, and range-product flow.

- **Vault.** Open-book reconstruction from flow, marked at fair value, answering the LP's real questions: concentration (HHI), the BTC move at which it breaches (a full repricing out to five sigma), worst historical drawdown, exit capacity against the withdrawal limiter, and one GREEN, AMBER or RED grade, exportable as a one-pager.

- **Managers.** The desk leaderboard: every account ranked by volume with realized and unrealized PnL, exposure, and open positions, with drill-through per desk.

- **Ops / Health.** One read on whether the market is trustworthy right now: oracle freshness against the staleness window and indexer lag against the chain head, under a single global verdict.

- **Cross-Venue.** Predict's ATM vol against Deribit's DVOL per tenor, the vol-risk-premium as Deribit implied minus Binance realized, and a DeepBook composability read framing the borrow-and-supply loop across Predict, `deepbook_margin` and DeepBook spot.

**It transacts, not just reads**

Where a read leads to an action, deepskew acts on-chain in place:

1. **Trade the surface.** A connect-gated ticket leads with the model-fair `N(d2)`, then mints or redeems a binary position against your own `PredictManager` with a real `predict::mint` or `predict::redeem`.

2. **Back the vault.** When the risk grade says the vault is safe, a panel supplies or withdraws dUSDC with a real `predict::supply` or `predict::withdraw`, clamped to the live withdrawal-limiter budget and free liquidity.

**Built on live state**

Every figure on screen is computed from current on-chain state. Nothing is sampled, mocked, or backfilled with placeholder data. If the chain has not produced it yet, deepskew does not show it. And it stays checkable: every fill and settlement row links to its transaction on Suiscan, and the status bar points straight at the package and source objects the desk reads.

**The math** is pure and runs in the browser from decoded on-chain parameters: raw SVI total variance and implied vol, Durrleman's butterfly plus the calendar arbitrage condition, digital pricing as `P(S_T > K) = N(d2)`, Breeden-Litzenberger risk-neutral density, 25-delta skew metrics, and a book-wide repricing to find the vault's breach point.

**Tech.** Next 16 (App Router, Turbopack) and React 19, TypeScript, Tailwind v4, shadcn/ui. Sui via the new `@mysten/dapp-kit-react` 2.0 on a gRPC client: reads through `client.core`, writes built with the `@mysten/sui` Transaction builder and signed through dApp Kit. TanStack Query over a typed indexer client. three / React Three Fiber for the 3D surface, lightweight-charts and hand-rolled SVG for the rest.

**Status.** Reads and transacts on Sui testnet today, where DeepBook Predict currently lives. The logic is network-agnostic, so deepskew follows Predict to mainnet at launch.

Live at [deepskew.xyz](http://deepskew.xyz)

About:

`# PredictOps Terminal

**One-line vision:** a Bloomberg-style operating terminal for DeepBook Predict BTC range markets.

PredictOps Terminal is a live trading terminal for **DeepBook Predict** on Sui testnet. It gives BTC range traders one workspace for market tape, volatility surface, payoff construction, guarded wallet execution, and post-trade position monitoring.

The product goal is to turn DeepBook Predict from a raw protocol surface into an operator-grade trading cockpit.

## Problem

DeepBook Predict introduces a powerful new market type: BTC range prediction markets priced from a volatility surface. The problem is that traders currently need to stitch together oracle state, SVI parameters, manager balances, range events, vault risk, and explorer links before they can operate with confidence. A terminal should answer execution questions immediately:

- Which BTC expiry is live and tradeable?
- What range am I expressing, and what is the premium/max payout profile?
- Did my transaction create, deposit, mint, or redeem correctly?
- Where is the DUSDC: wallet, manager, premium paid, or redeemable value?
- Is the range currently in band, settled, redeemed, or waiting for settlement?

PredictOps Terminal turns those protocol details into a focused trader workflow: select market, build payoff, sign guarded actions, then monitor lifecycle and PnL.

## What The App Does

- Loads live DeepBook Predict testnet markets from `predict-server.testnet.mystenlabs.com`.
- Shows active BTC oracle markets with readable expiry and time-left columns.
- Shows a live event tape from oracle price, SVI, trade, and range history.
- Lets traders choose a specific range from the ladder before building a ticket or minting.
- Shows a payoff builder with spot marker, center range, estimated premium, and max payout after premium.
- Builds a trade ticket from selected market + notional.
- Links package, predict object, selected oracle, manager, wallet, and transactions directly to SuiScan.
- Connects a Sui testnet wallet with Mysten dapp-kit.
- Detects the user's `PredictManager`.
- Supports testnet actions:
  - create `PredictManager`
  - deposit 5 DUSDC
  - mint a tiny 1 DUSDC range
  - redeem range positions back to the manager when available
- Shows portfolio state:
  - wallet DUSDC
  - manager DUSDC
  - account value
  - range positions
  - premium paid
  - current range PnL
- Shows each range mint with band, lifecycle, in-band/out-of-band, payout now, PnL now, settlement price, and tx links.
- Provides inline SVI tooltips without weakening the trader-first workflow.

## User Flow

1. Connect a Sui testnet wallet.
2. Pick an active BTC market.
3. Use the range simulator to inspect premium and risk.
4. Build a trade ticket.
5. Create or detect a `PredictManager`.
6. Deposit DUSDC into the manager.
7. Mint a tiny range.
8. Watch portfolio performance update in the terminal.
9. Redeem settled range positions when the action unlocks.`

About:

Predict Quant Suite is a professional analytics + trading stack built on DeepBook Predict, Sui's expiry-based, oracle-priced options/prediction primitive. It is three products that share one IV engine.

1. Surface Studio, a 3D volatility-surface viewer for every live Predict oracle. Toggle IV, delta, vega and gamma surfaces; cross-section the smile; read the implied-probability histogram; compare oracles side by side; track the ATM and 25-delta wing term structure against a realized-vs-implied volatility cone. The Markets tab renders the live on-chain orderbook with a BSM fair-value edge column (17 strikes priced in a single batched devInspect PTB), and the Activity tab streams real on-chain events with a contributor leaderboard and a day-of-week x hour heatmap. Everything is computed from live Gatheral SVI parameters pulled straight from chain, with a transparent chain-direct fallback when the public indexer 404s.

2. Vol-Arb bot, a keeper that prices the Predict IV surface against a Deribit-primary cross-feed (Polymarket fallback) every 15 seconds, surfaces arbitrage opportunities, and runs an optional Hyperliquid delta-hedge that computes per-position BSM delta and rebalances a perp when portfolio drift crosses a threshold. A backtest view replays the strategy over recorded spread history.

3. Telegram bot (@TheSmartPredictBot), the same trade path wrapped for non-crypto-native users. One tap claims a real testnet faucet (dUSDC + gas from a dedicated sponsor wallet), tap up or down on BTC, and the bot mints a real on-chain position; redeem cashes out to the user's own self-custody Sui wallet, exportable to Slush anytime. It only offers strikes the contract will actually mint, quoted live, and deep-links each user straight to their own positions in the web terminal.

The suite is not read-only marketing, it trades. The full mint path is live: create a PredictManager, deposit dUSDC, price a binary against the on-chain SVI surface, and mint a CALL/PUT position, all verified end-to-end on testnet. Redeem sweeps the payout back to the user's wallet. The web terminal, the keeper API and the bot all read one shared backend, so you analyze on the web and trade from Telegram against the exact same on-chain oracle.

Why DeepBook Predict and not a Polymarket clone: a Predict position is composable (levered via deepbook_margin, exited via Spot in one PTB), settles sub-400ms for game-like UX, is seeded by an internal market maker so there is no cold-start liquidity gap, and is vol-surface-priced across every strike and expiry rather than hand-listed binaries.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026. Mainnet redeploy planned for Predict mainnet day-one.

About:

FairLine — be the house of DeepBook.

A tranched, ML-risk-gated liquidity vault that earns the prediction-market edge and makes markets on DeepBook's core CLOB. Pick your risk (senior = protected, junior = leveraged); every number on-chain and verifiable.

The honest thesis
FairLine began as an ML-driven directional vault. In backtest it looked strong — live on testnet it lost real money: 41% win rate vs a 51.5% breakeven, −830 dUSDC, despite a 63%-accurate direction model. The 2% spread is wider than the edge. So we asked where the money went — on-chain, the answer is exact: into the house. We re-weighted FairLine from the losing player to the winning house, and repurposed the ML signal defensively: a strong predicted move is when the house carries the most risk, so we scale exposure down. A losing alpha model became a useful risk model.

Backtested over 500 real settled BTC oracles, being FairLine's risk-gated house returned +137% with a 9% max drawdown — vs −69% and an 85% drawdown for betting those same markets directionally.

What it is now — a structured product, not just a bot
• Senior / junior tranches — one deposit, your choice of risk. Senior (FLP-S) is principal-protected by junior's buffer with a steady capped yield (≤8% APR); junior (FLP-J) absorbs losses first and takes the leveraged upside. Structured credit applied to a prediction-market house.
• One risk brain, two venues — a single ML/volatility gate (logistic model, 12 features, 5-fold CV) drives a live GREEN/AMBER/RED posture (vol <30% → full exposure, 30–55% → 0.6×, ≥55% → 0.42× defensive floor) across both the DeepBook Predict house and a real limit-order maker on DeepBook's core CLOB (the whitelisted DEEP/SUI pool). The prediction market and the orderbook, one brain.
• Provably-fair pricing — deposits and withdrawals price at a freshly-marked NAV recomputed independently from the on-chain PLP rate, so you never enter or exit at a stale price.
• Capacity cap — the house edge is capacity-constrained, so an on-chain cap rejects deposits past productive capacity instead of silently diluting everyone's yield.
• Predictor rebate flywheel — routes a slice of the edge back to predictors pro-rata to real on-chain volume (live: 22 predictors paid in one PTB).

Our own Move contracts (live on testnet)
A tranched, NAV-based share vault: vault.move + flp_s/flp_j tranche tokens + a rewards flywheel, 11 passing unit tests, three clean package upgrades (capacity cap, rewards, then security hardening — an emergency pause and a 15% reserve floor so depositors always keep withdrawal liquidity). Composes atomically with Predict in one PTB, plus real place/cancel limit orders on DeepBook v3 through a BalanceManager. A 52-depositor simulation took it to real multi-user TVL.

Verify it yourself
Live dApp (Slush wallet): [fairline-vault.netlify.app](http://fairline-vault.netlify.app) — deposit into a tranche, receive FLP-S/FLP-J, withdraw pro-rata any time. Every cycle decision and transaction digest is logged; the dashboard shows live posture, fair-vs-on-chain price, tranche split, capacity, the flywheel pool, and the maker's resting orders. Testnet and unaudited — said plainly.

Why Sui / DeepBook
DeepBook Predict's 15-minute BTC binaries are a new on-chain primitive that needs a professionally-managed house; DeepBook's core CLOB needs liquidity. FairLine is the structured-product layer above both — turning house P&L into a risk-tranched, fair-priced share an ordinary person can hold without running a bot or taking uncapped downside.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026 — DeepBook track.

About:

**DeepEdge**
Don't bet blind. See the math — then check the machine.

DeepEdge is an AI agent that trades on DeepBook Predict, a market on Sui where you bet on questions like "will BTC be above $66,000 at expiry?"

Most trading bots ask you to trust them. DeepEdge works the other way around. You don't have to trust it — you can check everything it does. Every move it makes is capped on-chain, written down, and open for anyone to verify.

**The real problem**
Placing trades is the easy part. Lots of bots do that. The hard part is handing a machine your money and trusting it not to do something stupid. With most bots, all you have is the operator's word. DeepEdge gives you something better: proof.

**Two agents, and one can say no**
DeepEdge uses two AI agents.

The first one, the Strategist, looks at the market and suggests a bet. The second one, the Risk Officer, checks that suggestion — and can throw it out. Usually it does. So far it has rejected 28 of the last 30 bets.

That's not a bug. The Strategist keeps spotting "easy money" in places where the market has fooled people before, and the Risk Officer is there to catch it. Two minds, and the cautious one has the final say.

Before approving any bet, the Risk Officer checks four things:

How often the model has been right in the past.

Where the market itself expects the price to land.

Whether the pool that pays out winners is healthy.

Whether the on-chain price lines up with the rest of the market (Deribit, Binance).

If any of these says the bet is a bad idea, the Risk Officer writes down why, and passes.

**A spending limit it can't sneak around**
DeepEdge can only bet within limits set on the blockchain — we call this the Mandate. It's not a setting someone can quietly bump up later. The blockchain enforces it: the agent simply can't spend more than the cap per bet. The same limit covers every kind of bet it places — a simple up-or-down call, or a bet that the price lands inside a range.

And we didn't just try a few numbers to check. We proved it — mathematically, with a tool called Sui Prover — that no input, ever, lets the agent bet over the limit. Not "we tested it." Proven.

**Every decision is saved — and you can check it yourself**
Every time the agent acts — a bet, a rejection, or a pass — it saves the full reasoning, and locks a fingerprint of it onto the blockchain.

The Ledger screen lists every decision it has ever made. Click Verify on any one, and the page pulls up the original reasoning and re-checks the fingerprint, right in front of you. If a single word had been changed, it wouldn't match.

A bot says "trust me." DeepEdge says "check me" — and hands you the button.

It's also honest about what it's bad at. The Insights screen shows where the model has been wrong, including where it lost money. Knowing where not to trust itself is where its edge comes from.

**The tools a real trading desk would use**
DeepEdge reads the market the way a professional trading desk would — the same volatility curves, the same range structures the big players price by hand — except here every number is yours to check. And every one of these isn't just a chart; it feeds straight into the Risk Officer's decision:

A fair price for every betting level, from live market data.

The volatility curve, drawn fresh for each market.

The likely range where the price will land — and you can bet on that range directly, priced the same way Predict prices it, so the odds you see are the real ones.

A health grade for the payout pool (GREEN / AMBER / RED): can it cover the worst case, and can people get their money out?

A side-by-side check against Deribit and Binance, so you can see if the on-chain price is in line with the wider world.

**It really trades**
This isn't a demo with fake buttons. It works from start to finish on testnet: connect a wallet, see DeepEdge's fair price, pick UP or DOWN — or bet that the price lands inside a range — and place a real bet on-chain. Your positions and your wins and losses come straight from the blockchain. Real bets are tied to the reasoning behind them, there for anyone to see.

It also holds up on a bad day. If the data server stumbles, DeepEdge falls back to the last good data from the blockchain instead of crashing — still real, just a few moments behind.

**Why DeepBook Predict**
On Predict, every bet is priced live, settles fast, and plugs into the rest of Sui. DeepBook handles the plumbing. But someone still has to read the market — and keep a machine honest when it's betting for you.

About:

### **Phasis**

**Phasis is an on-chain options protocol on Sui. It brings the Deribit trading experience — a real central limit orderbook, cross-margin, and cash settlement — fully on-chain. Each option series trades on its own in-package central limit orderbook (a vendored DeepBook v3 matching engine), traders hold a single cross-margin account, and margin is computed from a portfolio-margin stress model. At expiry an off-chain crank halts trading, reads a signed Pyth Lazer settle price (or a DeepBook Predict oracle), and pays intrinsic value; undercollateralized accounts are liquidated by whitelisted keepers.

**

### **How it works**

- **Markets & series** — Admins list **markets** per `(underlying, expiry)` and **series** per `(strike, call/put)`. Each series carries its own **central limit orderbook embedded in the market object** (the DeepBook v3 matching engine, vendored in-package under `sources/book/`), quoted in USDC. Positions are tracked as **signed nets** on the account — there are no per-series coins or external pools.

- **Accounts** — Users hold one cross-margin **Account** (kept in an on-chain `AccountRegistry`, addressed by an `AccountCap`): free USDC + signed-net positions + a portfolio-margin lock. Long-only and debit spreads lock zero; naked shorts carry positive margin.

- **Margin model** — Portfolio margin from an **N-point Black–Scholes stress grid**, capturing PnL across a range of price scenarios rather than just the endpoints. Per-strike implied vol is sourced from the **DeepBook Predict SVI** surface.

- **Settlement** — At expiry, an off-chain **crank** halts trading and reads a settle price — a **signed Pyth Lazer** update pinned to the expiry second (`parse_and_verify_le_ecdsa_update`), or a **DeepBook Predict **`OracleSVI` — then fans out per user to pay intrinsic value. Whitelisted **keepers** liquidate undercollateralized accounts.

- **Stack** — On-chain Move (`phasis`, with the DeepBook v3 matching engine vendored in `sources/book/`), a Rust keeper layer (stress-publisher, cranker, liquidator, market-maker) that reads via gRPC/GraphQL and submits PTBs, a `@phasis-lab/sdk` TypeScript binding layer, and a Next.js + dApp-Kit frontend.
